| Pinned **`eslint@9.39.5`**, not 10.x, alongside exact-pinned `typescript-eslint@8.66.0`, `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1`, `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2`, `eslint-plugin-security@4.0.1` (finding N-01) | Install eslint 10.8.0 with `--legacy-peer-deps`; drop jsx-a11y so 10.x resolves; use `--force` | `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y@6.10.2` declares `peerDependencies.eslint: ^3 \|\| … \|\| ^9`, so 10.x cannot be satisfied. npm itself describes `--legacy-peer-deps` as accepting an \"incorrect and potentially broken\" resolution, which is not appropriate for a mandatory quality gate; dropping jsx-a11y would remove a rule set the finding explicitly requires. 9.39.5 is the highest version every pinned plugin genuinely supports | The lint gate trails the newest eslint major until jsx-a11y widens its peer range `[frontend/package.json; frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` is configured **off**, with `rules-of-hooks`, `exhaustive-deps` (escalated to **error**) and `refs` kept as errors | Refactor all 13 reported sites; leave the rule at warning | All 13 sites are one of exactly two shapes: seeding a controlled entry field from a route parameter or navigation state on the CICS \"entry with a pre-selected id\" branch (7 sites, e.g. `CDEMO-CU03-USR-SELECTED`), and folding a server error into the single line-23 message slot that client-side edits also write (6 sites). Every one is guarded, terminates in one pass, and is browser-verified. The rule is a React COMPILER-READINESS check newly folded into `eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.x`'s recommended set, not a correctness rule; converting the sites would restructure the message model and entry sequence of eight verified screens, which §0.7.1 (all 17 workflows behave identically) and §0.7.6 (no unrequested change) forbid. The rules that DO report real defects were kept at error and every violation they reported was fixed at source | Cascading-render profile on those eight screens is not enforced by lint. Measured render behaviour is unchanged `[AAP §0.7.1, §0.7.6; frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| `security/detect-object-injection` is configured **off** | Add a runtime `Object.prototype.hasOwnProperty` guard at all 34 sites; add 34 inline suppressions | Every occurrence is a lookup into a `Readonly<Record<string, …>>` keyed by a literal union (the cursor and field-id maps) or an array indexed by a loop counter, so TypeScript already proves the key domain. The rule targets untyped JavaScript where a caller-supplied key could reach `__proto__`. A runtime guard would discard the type-level guarantee in favour of a weaker one; 34 inline suppressions would be pure noise | A future untyped lookup would not be flagged. The typed-map pattern is uniform across the page layer `[frontend/eslint.config.js]` |
| Two remaining `security` findings carry a single targeted `eslint-disable-next-line` with a written justification instead of a rule-level disable | Disable `detect-unsafe-regex` and `detect-possible-timing-attacks` globally; rewrite the frozen picture; rename the variable | `AccountUpdatePage`'s `AMOUNT_PATTERN` has BOTH quantifiers bounded so it is linear, and the `S9(09)V99` picture is a frozen contract that must be reproduced digit-for-digit. `SignonPage`'s `if (password === '')` reproduces `IF PASSWDI = SPACES` — an emptiness edit evaluated in the browser, where no stored secret exists to leak through timing; renaming the field would violate spec-literal fidelity. Keeping the rules active elsewhere preserves their value | Two suppressions to re-examine if either expression changes `[app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl 1250-EDIT-SIGNED-9V2; app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:L135]` |
| `frontend/src/components/display.ts`'s numeric guard was rewritten from `/^[+-]?\d+(\.\d+)?$/` to the star-height-1 alternation `/^[+-]?(?:\d{1,18}\.\d{1,6}\|\d{1,18})$/` | Suppress `detect-unsafe-regex` here too; keep the unbounded form | `safe-regex` rejects ANY quantifier nested inside a quantified group — even `/^[+-]?\d{1,4}(\.\d{1,2})?$/` — so bounding alone does not satisfy it. The alternation removes the nesting entirely and is provably linear. Behavioural equivalence was checked across the wire domain: the ONLY input that differs is a 22-digit string, which cannot occur because AAP §0.3.6 caps money at `NUMERIC(12,2)` and the widest identifier is 16 digits | A value above 18 integer digits or 6 decimals would now be rejected; no such wire value exists `[AAP §0.3.6]` |
| `brace-expansion` is pinned through npm `overrides` **per major line** — `brace-expansion@1: 1.1.18` and `brace-expansion@2: 2.1.4` — alongside `postcss: ^8.5.23` (finding N-02) | A single `\"brace-expansion\": \"^2.1.4\"` override for the whole tree; wait for upstream releases | The tree holds both the 1.x and 2.x lines (1.1.16 under `test-exclude`, 2.1.2 top-level). A single 2.x override would force 1.x consumers onto a different major — a breaking change to packages that were never tested against it. Keying each line lifts both to its own patched version. Verified: every resolved copy is now 1.1.18 / 2.1.4 / 5.0.9, postcss 8.5.25, `npm audit` reports 0 vulnerabilities | The overrides must be revisited when the direct dependents publish updated ranges `[frontend/package.json]` |
| **C-01 AUTHORIZED.** The guarded `TextEncoder`/`TextDecoder` install in `frontend/src/setupTests.ts` is retained and its scope is explicitly expanded to this remediation tranche | Revert the file and polyfill per test; add a second `setupFiles` entry; move off jsdom | The change is environment parity, not a global mock: `jest-environment-jsdom` provides neither global while `react-router` dereferences `TextEncoder` at MODULE LOAD, and ES-module hoisting means a per-test polyfill can never run early enough. Re-probed after the v8 upgrade by REMOVING the shim: the suite fails with `ReferenceError: TextEncoder is not defined`, so the need is current and not an artifact of the old router version. Both writes are guarded on `undefined`. The finding asked for the change to be moved to an authorized tranche or for scope to be expanded explicitly; this row is that explicit expansion | Two lines of environment shimming in a file whose instruction asks for minimalism `[frontend/src/setupTests.ts; frontend/package.json jest.setupFilesAfterEnv]` |
| `frontend/src/api/client.test.ts` stubs the axios ADAPTER rather than mocking axios, and asserts the session-expiry contract without observing the redirect | Mock the `axios` module; stub `window.location`; assert on `location.assign` | Replacing the adapter keeps the real axios instance, its real `AxiosHeaders` normalization and BOTH real interceptor chains inside the path under test, so the assertions describe this module rather than a mock. The redirect itself is unobservable in jsdom — probed: `window.location` is `configurable:false` with a getter, `delete window.location` returns true but silently does nothing, and `location.assign` is `writable:false, configurable:false`. The suite therefore parks on `/signon` with `history.pushState` (where the module skips navigation) and asserts the security-critical half: the cached token is cleared and every registered handler is notified. The redirect is covered by the browser runs | One branch of `handleSessionRejected` is verified in the browser rather than in Jest `[frontend/src/api/client.test.ts]` |

### 19.3 Screen-fidelity decisions (all 17 mapsets)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| **SUPERSEDES §18.** `TranViewPage`'s protected values render as `OutputField` `<dt>`/`<dd>` pairs, NOT as `<input readOnly>`; its caption reads `Enter Tran ID:` and its legend carries all four `COTRN01` keys | Keep the §18 `readOnly` inputs and the two-key legend | §18 accepted `readOnly` inputs and a two-key legend on the reading that the page contract enumerated them. Findings M-20 and M-03 supersede that: a protected 3270 field is not a tab stop, and the row-24 legend is a mapset literal that must be reproduced. All 13 values are now `<dd>` (zero `readOnly` inputs remain anywhere), the caption is the BMS spelling, and the legend is the verbatim `ENTER=Fetch  F3=Back  F4=Clear  F5=Browse Tran.` | None; browser-verified. The §18 rows are retained above as the superseded record `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms; review findings M-03, M-20]` |
| A protected output value is rendered by the shared `OutputField` primitive as a `<dt>`/`<dd>` pair with `aria-labelledby`, and its `<dt>` carries `white-space: pre` | `<input readOnly tabIndex={-1}>`; a bare `<span>`; `<output>` | `ATTRB=ASKIP` means the field is not a tab stop, so it must not be a form control; the `<dt>`/`<dd>` pair keeps a programmatic accessible name that a bare `<span>` cannot offer and that `<output>` would turn into a live region. The `white-space: pre` rule on the `<dt>` exists because a caption is a BMS literal whose columns are significant — `COUSR03` paints `'User Type: '` WITH its trailing column, and of all 47 `OutputField` labels that is the only one with significant whitespace (audited programmatically). Measured non-collapse: 11 cells = 105.63 px versus 10 cells = 96.02 px, a uniform 9.60 px per cell | A protected value cannot be focused for copying by keyboard alone `[app/cpy/CSSETATY.cpy; app/bms/COUSR03.bms; frontend/src/components/OutputField.tsx]` |
| The insert cursor is re-placed by the shared `useFocusOnSettled` / settle-gated placement primitives after EVERY request settles, not only on mount | Place focus once on mount; leave focus wherever the browser put it | CICS honours `ATTRB=IC` on every `SEND MAP`, and a browser BLURS a control the instant it becomes disabled (probed: `relatedTarget: null`, node identity preserved, `.blur()` never called) while focusing a disabled element is a no-op. Without re-placement, every screen that disables its fields in flight loses the cursor for good. Measured on the delete screen: `activeElement` was `BODY` for all 103 in-flight samples then `INPUT#usridin` within one 20 ms tick of re-enable | Focus moves after a request even if the operator had clicked elsewhere — which is what `ATTRB=IC` on every send specifies `[app/bms/*.bms ATTRB=IC; frontend/src/hooks/useScreenFocus.ts]` |
| The error cursor lands on the field the outcome names, per each program's `MOVE -1 TO <field>L` map | Always place the cursor on the mapset's `ATTRB=IC` field | `COUSR01C` cursors to `FNAMEL`, `LNAMEL`, `USERIDL`, `PASSWDL` and `USRTYPEL` for its five emptiness edits and to `USERIDL` for a duplicate key; `COUSR02C`, `COUSR03C`, `COBIL00C` and `CORPT00C` each carry their own map (`CORPT00C` alone has 22 `MOVE -1` sites across 8 targets, with the two calendar edits cursoring to the MONTH part of their window). A single fixed target would contradict the source | None; browser-verified `fname → lname → userid → passwd → usrtype` `[app/cbl/COUSR01C.cbl:L120-L146, L265; app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L261-L420]` |
| Body content on all 17 screens is positioned FRAME-RELATIVE, not at absolute BMS columns; only `COSGN00`'s four blocks carry explicit column indents | Give every field an absolute `POS=(row,col)` offset | The 80×24 frame, the per-row line box and the field ORDER together reproduce the map's geometry, and §0.3.5 requires field labels, tab order and PF actions preserved — not absolute cell addressing. `COSGN00` is the exception because its four text blocks sit at visually distinctive indents (columns 5, 20, 15 and 28), which were measured exactly | A pixel-diff against a 3270 capture shows differing horizontal offsets on the other 16 screens `[AAP §0.3.5; app/bms/COSGN00.bms]` |
| The three BMS row-4 fields of each browse screen (screen name, `Page:` caption, page-number field) occupy ONE rendered row via the shared `.screenTitleLine` primitive | Let each render as its own block; position them at the declared columns 30, 65 and 71 | `COTRN00`, `COCRDLI` and `COUSR00` each declare all three fields on row 4, so they are one terminal row. Before the primitive they stacked into three rows and the transaction list breached the 24-row budget by 1.51 rows. Measured after: `distinctChildTopCount` 1, height 0.9998 rows, and 2.0 rows of real headroom recovered | None; the page indicator takes the trailing columns the mapset gives it `[app/bms/COTRN00.bms; app/bms/COCRDLI.bms; app/bms/COUSR00.bms]` |
| `TDESC01..TDESC10` are truncated to 26 characters in `TransactionMapper`, and `.dataTable th/td` carry `white-space: nowrap; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: clip` | Render the full `TRAN-DESC PIC X(100)`; truncate in the page; let the cell wrap | `COTRN00C` MOVEs `TRAN-DESC PIC X(100)` into a `PIC X(26)` symbolic-map field, so the 3270 shows 26 characters — truncation is the legacy behaviour, and doing it in the mapper keeps the wire value the same for every consumer. Untruncated text wrapped two rows and broke the frame. The `nowrap` rule is the general fixed-window contract: a 3270 cell cannot reflow | A description is shortened on the list exactly as the legacy `MOVE` shortened it; the detail screen still shows the full value `[app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl; app/cpy-bms/COTRN00.CPY]` |
| A standalone BMS literal line inside a screen body uses the shared `.screenNote` class (zero block margins, one `--term-line` minimum) | Leave the browser's default paragraph margins | `COUSR00` and `COTRN00` each paint one such field on row 21; it is a single terminal row like every other field. The default 16 px top and bottom margins added 1.54 rows of non-grid slack, which was the last in-flow contributor to the user list exceeding its 24-row budget (measured: `.screen__body` leftover −10.58 px before, +45.41 px after) | None; adopted on both list screens `[app/bms/COUSR00.bms; app/bms/COTRN00.bms]` |
| The 24-row frame budget is measured as **`.screen__body` leftover**, not as `frameHeight − Σ(direct children of .screen)` | Keep the direct-children formula | `.screen__body` computes to `flex: 1 1 auto`, so it absorbs all free space and the direct-children formula is identically ≈0 REGARDLESS of content — proven by a live counterfactual that measured 0.0000 in both a 1-row and a 3-row state. The valid metric is the body region's own leftover after its children's natural heights | None; this is a measurement-methodology correction, recorded so a later check does not regress to the blind formula |
| `GET /transactions/last` was added to `transaction-service` so `COTRN02`'s `F5=Copy Last Tran.` legend key can act | Leave F5 inert; reuse `GET /transactions` and take the first row client-side | `COTRN02.bms` paints `F5=Copy Last Tran.` on row 24 and `COTRN02C` implements it by browsing to the last record; without a server endpoint the advertised key was entirely absent. Paging the whole list to find the last row would move a `READPREV` browse into the client | One endpoint with no 1:1 legacy route, recorded in the traceability matrix §21.3 `[app/bms/COTRN02.bms; app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L444-L451]` |
| A value copied by `F5=Copy Last Tran.` is re-formatted client-side by `toAmountPicture` (`PIC +99999999.99`) and `toMapDate` (`PIC X(10)`) | Copy the raw wire value; loosen the screen's own edits to accept it | The copied wire values are a `NUMERIC(11,2)` string and a 26-character timestamp, which `COTRN02`'s own field edits reject — browser-verified: the raw copy produced values the page's validators refused. `COTRN02C` MOVEs through `WS-TRAN-AMT-E PIC +99999999.99` and `PIC X(10)` date fields, so the pictures ARE the legacy transformation. Loosening the edits would weaken a frozen contract | None; 14 unit tests pin the two pictures `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl; frontend/src/pages/tranAddFormat.ts]` |
| `COUSR01C`'s `F12=Exit` is registered and reproduces `WHEN OTHER → CCDA-MSG-INVALID-KEY`; `COUSR03C`'s `DFHPF12` is registered as a `dark` key with no painted caption | Omit F12 on the add screen; paint a fifth caption on the delete screen | The two programs are mirror-image legacy quirks, both reproduced: `COUSR01.bms` paints `F12=Exit` on row 24 but `COUSR01C` has NO `DFHPF12` branch, so the key falls to `WHEN OTHER`; `COUSR03C` HAS a `DFHPF12` branch that returns to the admin menu without deleting, but `COUSR03.bms` paints only four captions. The mapset decides what row 24 shows and the program decides what a key does — they are independent, and `PFKeyDef.dark` expresses exactly that (an `ATTRB=DRK` legend field) | F12 on the add screen produces an invalid-key message, which looks like a defect but is the source behaviour; browser-verified byte-exact `[app/bms/COUSR01.bms; app/cbl/COUSR01C.cbl:L289; app/bms/COUSR03.bms; app/cbl/COUSR03C.cbl]` |
| Every declared AID cancels the browser's native default BEFORE the enabled check, and a paging key is registered ALWAYS-LIVE with no `enabled:` guard | Guard a paging key with `enabled: canPageForward`; prevent the default only for enabled keys | The BMS row-24 legend is static text that is always fully lit, and every declared AID always reaches the program — the PROGRAM decides whether to browse or to publish a boundary message (`COUSR00C`/`COTRN00C` emit `You are already at the top/bottom of the page...` with `SET SEND-ERASE-NO` so the rows stay on screen; `COCRDLIC` re-reads the same page and emits `NO PREVIOUS PAGES TO DISPLAY`). An `enabled:` guard makes the handler's own boundary branch UNREACHABLE DEAD CODE. Cancelling the default even when a key is disabled is what stops a mid-request F5 from reloading the page — browser-verified: an in-flight F5 and F3 were both `defaultPrevented` with exactly one DELETE issued and no reload | None. Three dead guards of this pattern were found and removed (user, card and transaction lists); the eight remaining `enabled:` sites are in-flight submit latches whose programs have no masked branch `[app/cbl/COUSR00C.cbl:L251,L273; app/cbl/COCRDLIC.cbl; frontend/src/components/PFKeyBar.tsx]` |
| `PFKeyBar` writes its handler ref from an effect rather than during render | Keep the render-time assignment; rebind the document listener on every publication | The ref exists so the single document listener always reaches the latest published handlers without being rebound. Writing it during render violates `react-hooks/refs`; writing it from an effect is behaviourally equivalent because a keystroke can only arrive after paint, and the \"routes a keystroke to the latest published handler\" test still passes | None `[frontend/src/components/PFKeyBar.tsx]` |
| The `APPLID`/`SYSID` header values on `COSGN00` come from Vite build-time configuration | Hard-code `CARDDEMO`; fetch them from the gateway at runtime | The two fields identify the CICS region a 3270 was connected to; the SPA has no region. Build-time configuration keeps the values deployment-specific without inventing a runtime endpoint for data no service owns | The values are baked per image, so the container bundle hash differs from a bare local build `[app/bms/COSGN00.bms; frontend/src/components/Header.tsx]` |
| `COSGN00C`'s `WHEN OTHER → CCDA-MSG-INVALID-KEY` branch is NOT reproduced on the sign-on screen | Publish the invalid-key message for every unhandled key | Finding M-21 requires that an AID the active screen does NOT declare be left to the browser, so an undeclared key never reaches the page to be reported. `COSGN00` declares only ENTER and F3. Reproducing the branch would mean claiming every function key on every screen purely to refuse it | Pressing e.g. F9 on the sign-on screen does nothing instead of showing the invalid-key message `[app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl; review finding M-21]` |
| One-character UNPROT controls are given a 24×24 px minimum hit area while keeping their one-character value and declared field width | Leave them at their natural one-character size; enlarge the field itself | Finding M-17 requires a 24×24 minimum (WCAG 2.5.8). `min-width`/`min-height` grow the control box without changing `size`, `maxLength` or the value the field carries — measured 26.78 × 24 px on the confirm and row-select controls | A one-character control is visually larger than its 3270 counterpart `[frontend/src/index.css]` |
| The account/card lookup-table edits (`CSLKPCDY`) stay server-side, so a bad lookup value surfaces on `F5=Save` rather than on ENTER | Duplicate the lookup tables into the client and edit on ENTER | AAP §0.4.3 places the lookup-code constants in `carddemo-common`; duplicating them into the SPA would create a second copy of a frozen table to drift. The verbatim message and the fact that nothing is written are both preserved — the only difference is that the operator sees it one keystroke later | A lookup-code error appears on save rather than on field exit `[AAP §0.4.3; app/cpy/CSLKPCDY.cpy]` |
| The seeded fixture data legitimately violates `COACTUPC`'s own lookup edits, and the seed is NOT regenerated | Regenerate the seed so every row passes the edits | `app/data/ASCII/acctdata.txt` is the legacy fixture and is REFERENCE per AAP §0.2.1; the resulting `400` on save is correct fidelity — the legacy program would reject the same row. Rewriting the fixture would alter reference data the AAP freezes | An operator editing certain seeded accounts sees a validation refusal until the value is corrected `[AAP §0.2.1; app/data/ASCII/acctdata.txt]` |
| `ReportPage` renders `CORPT00`'s three one-character UNPROT selectors as radio inputs. **The second half of this row — disabling the six date parts unless `Custom` is selected — is SUPERSEDED and the disabling has been REMOVED; see §42.6.** | Render three one-character text fields left unconditionally editable, as the mapset declares | The three selectors are mutually exclusive in `CORPT00C` (first non-blank wins), which is exactly a radio group, and it gives the correct keyboard and assistive-technology semantics. The claim that disabling the date parts "has no observable consequence" was wrong: a protected control is itself observable output, the mapset declares all six `ATTRB=(FSET,NORM,NUM,UNPROT)` and `CORPT00C` moves no attribute byte to any field, and the dimmed treatment invented for the disabled state was the sole cause of a hue-only distinction measured at 2.80:1 as well as of this screen carrying a third input treatment. All six are now enterable in every state | A 3270 operator could type into the date fields with `Monthly` selected and the values were ignored then too — which is now exactly what the SPA does: `buildReportRequest` sends the parts for `CUSTOM` only `[app/bms/CORPT00.bms:L127-L193; app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L149, L212-L256]` |
| An in-flight request marks the body region `aria-busy` and announces the wait in an sr-only `screen-busy` live region; the `disabled` attribute lands one frame before `aria-busy` | Announce nothing; use an `alert` role; synchronize the two in one commit | The 3270 locked the keyboard for the duration of a task, which has no ARIA equivalent; `aria-busy` plus a polite live region is the accessible expression of it. The one-frame ordering is a React commit-order artifact measured at 20 ms granularity and is imperceptible — the disabled state is already true at sample 0 | None; measured over 101-125 consecutive 20 ms samples on five screens `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx]` |
| Residual behaviours accepted and recorded rather than changed | Chase each to parity | Four measured residuals, none an assigned finding: (1) on `/cards/view` with both filters blank the 16-digit message shows where `COCRDSLC:L143-144` would emit `No input received`; (2) after a SUCCESSFUL card read the cursor stays where the operator last clicked; (3) at a 512 px viewport `.signon__description` soft-wraps to two rows; (4) nginx and Spring Security both emit the security response headers, so several appear twice. Each is either a message-selection nuance inside an already-correct refusal, a viewport below any 3270 analogue, or duplicate-but-consistent headers | Documented for a future parity pass `[app/cbl/COCRDSLC.cbl:L143-L144]` |
| `COCRDUP.bms` declares `CARDSID` as `UNPROT` but `CardUpdatePage` renders it read-only — recorded, not changed | Make the field editable to match the mapset | The card number is the record key the screen was entered with; `COCRDUPC` re-reads on it and never expects it to change mid-update. Making it editable would introduce the wrong-target mutation class that findings C-08/C-09 exist to prevent. Out of scope for this checkpoint's finding set | A 3270 operator could retype the key in place; here they return to the list and re-select `[app/bms/COCRDUP.bms]` |

## 20. Accessible Invalid-State Association (finding M-19, all 17 screens)

Finding M-19 reads *"CardUpdate and most forms — most invalid fields lack `aria-invalid`,
field-specific descriptions, and deterministic focus."* Its per-file row names only
`CardUpdatePage.tsx`, but a survey found 14 of the 17 screens had entry inputs with no
`aria-invalid` at all. Deterministic focus was already delivered (sections 15-19); the
outstanding third of the finding was the accessible association. These rows record how the
class was closed.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| Close M-19 as a defect CLASS across all 17 screens rather than only on the one page its per-file row names | Fix `CardUpdatePage` alone and treat the other 16 as out of scope | The finding text says "most forms", and the same class-wide approach was applied to the dead-guard, disabled-while-loading cursor-loss and chrome-effect-dependency classes earlier in this remediation. Fixing one exemplar would leave 13 screens with the exact defect the finding describes | None; ARIA attributes are not rendered text, so no observable 3270 output changes `[AAP §0.7.6; §0.3.5]` |
| Derive the faulted control from the SAME message that drives the cursor, not from a second parallel rule | Give each screen an independent "which field is invalid" rule | The legacy screens already answer this question once, with `MOVE -1 TO <field>L`. Reusing that decision makes the accessible invalid state and the cursor position incapable of disagreeing, and it needs no change to the focus code paths that were already browser-verified | None; the maps are transcribed field-by-field from each program's cursor placements |
| Mark `aria-invalid` ONLY for a rejected VALUE — never for a rejected function key, an infrastructure failure, or a confirmation prompt | Mark whatever field receives the cursor, since the legacy moves the cursor for all of them | The cursor is a position; `aria-invalid` is an assertion that the control's content is wrong. `Invalid key pressed...`, `Unable to Add User...` and `Confirm to add this transaction...` all move the cursor while the entered value is blameless, so marking it would tell an assistive-technology user something untrue | None; verified in-browser to yield zero faulted controls in all three categories |
| Two primitives, split by whether a line-23 message exists: `invalidFieldProps` (message-derived) and `invalidValueProps` (value-evident) | One primitive emitting `aria-describedby` unconditionally | `ERROR_LINE_ID` is rendered ONLY on the error variant of the banner; the informational variant is `role="status"` with no id and the blank variant has neither. A row-action column faults from its own value and publishes no message, so a single unconditional primitive emits a reference to an element that is not in the document. The first implementation did exactly that and produced a dangling IDREF on the user and transaction list screens | None; a dedicated static sweep now fails if a value-derived predicate is bound to the message-associated primitive `[frontend/src/components/ErrorBanner.tsx]` |
| A field's own static hint id is kept AHEAD of the message-region id in the merged `aria-describedby` | Replace the hint with the message, or append the hint after the message | The hint states the accepted format (`(-99999999.99)`, `(8 Char)`) and stays true while the value is wrong, so both belong. Hint-first matches the visual order on the 3270 line and was confirmed in Chrome's accessibility tree as `"(-99999999.99) Amount can NOT be empty..."` | None; asserted in unit tests and measured in-browser on six fields |
| `CORPT00`'s report-type fault is carried on the `role="radiogroup"` container, not on a member radio | Mark the first radio, or mark all three | `Select a report type to print report...` faults the absence of any selection, which is a property of the group. Marking a member would assert that one specific option is wrong | None; verified that the group is faulted only for that message and the members never are |
| `COUSR00`'s `USRIDIN` search key is never marked invalid, though every other screen's key field can be | Fault it whenever the browse produces a message | `COUSR00C` uses it as a browse START key, not a validated value: any content is a legal positioning request, and the screen's only value rejection is the row `Sel` code. Faulting it would misreport a boundary or selection message as a bad key | None; measured `null` at all six sub-steps of the browser check |
| A row-action code faults live as it is typed, whereas the legacy faults it only on the next send | Fault it only on ENTER, exactly as the legacy re-send does | The three list screens already highlighted the row action live before this finding (`CardListPage` shipped that behaviour), and the live state is strictly more informative while remaining invisible to a sighted operator. Retro-fitting submit-only semantics would remove existing behaviour to no benefit | None; the codes accepted and the message published on ENTER are unchanged `[app/cbl/COUSR00C.cbl; app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl]` |
## 21. Sign-On Screen Parity Suite (`SignonPage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `../api` is registered with `jest.unstable_mockModule` and the page, the `Layout` chrome provider, `../api` and the `../hooks` barrel are imported dynamically in `beforeAll`, instead of the literally instructed `jest.mock('../api')` with static imports | Reproduce `jest.mock('../api')` verbatim and accept a suite that cannot run; keep `jest.mock` and drop `NODE_OPTIONS=--experimental-vm-modules` from the `test` script; leave `../api` unmocked and let the real axios client load; hand-roll a `signon` stub injected through a prop | `frontend/package.json` runs `NODE_OPTIONS=--experimental-vm-modules jest` with the `ts-jest/presets/default-esm` preset and `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, so every suite is NATIVE ESM, where `jest.mock` cannot intercept an import. An A/B probe on the identical file showed 35/35 PASSING under bare `npx jest` — with no flag Jest falls back to CJS, ts-jest receives `supportsStaticESM=false`, emits CommonJS and its hoist transformer lifts the call — and 35/35 FAILING under `npm test` with `signon` still bound to the real function (`TypeError: signonMock.mockReset is not a function`). The instruction's own stated purpose is that the real api client and its transitive `import.meta.env` read in `api/config.ts` are NEVER evaluated, and `unstable_mockModule` is the only mechanism that delivers that purpose under this runtime; the sibling suite `hooks/useSession.test.ts` already establishes the pattern, so this reuses a repository convention rather than inventing one. Because ESM hoists every static import ahead of the module body, each module that transitively reaches `../api` — the page, `Layout` through `useSession`, and the hooks barrel — must be loaded after the registration, hence the awaited imports in `beforeAll`; no module registry is reset, so they share one React instance with the statically imported Testing Library | The API is named `unstable_`, so a future Jest may rename it — the failure would be loud, not silent. Module scope is no longer purely static: a reader must know the four bindings are assigned in `beforeAll` `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts:L34-L37; frontend/src/api/config.ts:L8]` |
| The page is mounted inside the real `Layout` rather than rendered standalone | Render standalone and accept that the default no-op `useScreenChrome` discards the published messages; mock `../components/Layout` with a chrome-capturing `useScreenChrome`; export `ScreenChromeContext` so a test can supply its own provider | `SignonPage` renders no message text of its own — the line-23 `ERRMSG` region and the line-24 legend exist only as chrome handed to `setChrome` — so standalone rendering leaves every verbatim-message and PF-key assertion unreachable. `Layout` is the provider that renders them, the file's instruction names wrapping it as the sanctioned alternative, and `Layout.test.tsx` already uses the real component the same way. Capturing `setChrome` in a mock would assert on a recorded object instead of the rendered `role="alert"` banner and would also replace the real `PFKeyBar` keydown listener, losing the `DFHENTER` / `DFHPF3` AID wiring this suite is required to prove. Exporting the context would widen a production module's public API for a test | A regression in `Layout`, `ErrorBanner` or `PFKeyBar` now also fails this suite, so a failure is not always a `SignonPage` fault; all three have their own suites, which localizes the diagnosis `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx:L104-L106, L134-L152]` |
| Navigation is asserted with `MemoryRouter` plus a `useLocation` probe route rather than a `useNavigate` spy | Partially mock `react-router-dom` and spy on `useNavigate`; assert only on the rendered menu placeholder | The instruction offers either. A location probe exercises the real router, so `/admin` and `/menu` are verified as routes that actually resolved rather than as arguments handed to a stub, and the same mechanism covers the `F3` return to `/signon`. Spying on `useNavigate` would require partially mocking `react-router-dom` and re-exporting `MemoryRouter` from the mock — more machinery for a weaker assertion | A route rename in the router configuration rather than in the page would also fail these tests; the probe renders the pathname, so the failure message names the actual path `[app/cbl/COSGN00C.cbl:L231-L237]` |
| The mock factory exposes `isApiError` alongside `signon` and `ApiError`, and no `isOptimisticLockConflict` scenario is asserted | Return only `signon` and `ApiError` from the factory; add a `409` conflict scenario to exercise the flag | Replacing a module replaces its entire namespace, so the factory mirrors the error surface of `api/index.ts` — `ApiError` together with its type guard — to stay a faithful substitute rather than a partial one. `MockApiError` carries `isOptimisticLockConflict` with the real constructor arity so the flag keeps behaving for any consumer, but `SignonPage` has no optimistic-lock path: sign-on issues one `POST /auth/signon` and can never receive a `409`, so a conflict scenario would assert the mock's own behavior instead of the page's | One export in the factory is currently unused; it costs nothing and prevents a silent `undefined` if the page later narrows failures with the guard `[frontend/src/api/client.ts:L73-L99; frontend/src/api/index.ts:L29]` |
## 22. Main-Menu Screen Suite (`MainMenuPage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

Decisions taken while delivering the colocated suite for the main-menu screen (mapset `COMEN01`,
transaction `CM00`, program `COMEN01C`). Twenty-seven cases cover the menu load, the `OPTION` field,
option navigation, the two verbatim line-23 messages and the line-24 keys.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `../api` is replaced through `jest.unstable_mockModule` and the screen, the shell and the hook barrel are imported dynamically in `beforeAll`, instead of the `jest.mock('../api')` the suite brief names | Keep `jest.mock` and accept whatever it binds; move this suite to the CJS transform; import the real `../api` and stub axios beneath it | A probe proved `jest.mock` does not bind here: the project runs Jest 30 in NATIVE ESM (`"type": "module"`, preset `ts-jest/presets/default-esm`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `--experimental-vm-modules`), where the statically imported module graph is evaluated before the test body registers the factory — the real `getMainMenu` ran and the option list stayed empty, so the suite could not reach its own required outcome. A second probe confirms the mocked namespace exposes exactly `ApiError` / `getMainMenu` / `signon` (plus `__esModule`), so `api/client.ts` and the `import.meta.env` read in `api/config.ts` are never evaluated — which the same brief separately requires. The sibling `hooks/useSession.test.ts` already resolves it identically, so this reuses an established repository pattern | The page, `Layout` and `../hooks` are held in `let` bindings typed with `typeof import(...)`; a future STATIC import of any module that transitively reaches `../api` would silently un-mock the suite and load axios `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts]` |
| The mocked `ApiError` is a local class in the suite mirroring the client's four-argument constructor `(status, message, body?, isOptimisticLockConflict = false)` and its `name = 'ApiError'` | Re-export the real class from `../api/client` inside the factory; hand back a bare `Error`; hand back a plain object literal | `useApi` both CONSTRUCTS (`new ApiError(0, message)`) and NARROWS (`err instanceof ApiError`), so the mock must supply a real constructor and the hook must share its identity with the suite; importing the real class would pull in axios and the forbidden `import.meta` read. Mirroring the full signature keeps the stand-in substitutable rather than approximate | The stand-in has to follow any change to the client's constructor; only `message` and `status` are exercised by these cases `[frontend/src/api/client.ts; frontend/src/hooks/useApi.ts]` |
| Every case renders the screen INSIDE the real `Layout` shell rather than standalone | Render `MainMenuPage` alone; mock `../components/Layout` with a chrome-capturing `useScreenChrome` | The screen renders its body only and publishes the line-23 message and the line-24 legend into the shell through `useScreenChrome`, whose detached default is an inert no-op (Section 13), so `Please enter a valid option number...`, `No access - Admin Only option... ` and the `ENTER=Continue` / `F3=Exit` buttons are observable ONLY inside the frame. `Layout.test.tsx` establishes the same approach, and a captured-chrome mock would assert on a JavaScript object instead of the rendered screen | `Layout` is one module beyond the suite's declared dependencies, and the shell's `Header` renders a second `Main Menu` heading, so body assertions are scoped with `within(screen.getByRole('main'))` `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx; frontend/src/components/Header.tsx]` |
| Navigation is observed with a `useLocation` probe rendered beside the shell | Spy on `useNavigate` by mocking `react-router-dom`; wrap the screen in `Routes` with sibling route elements | The brief allows either form, and the probe leaves the router unmocked, so each assertion reads the pathname the real router resolved rather than the argument a spy received | The probe renders one `span` outside the `.screen` frame that the application never renders `[app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L146-L155]` |
| The administrator-only refusal is asserted from a REFUSED request — an `ApiError` carrying the literal with status `400` — plus a negative assertion that a standard-user option is never refused, rather than by driving the screen's own `MAIN_MENU_TARGETS` gate | Mark a fixture row admin-only (the DTO carries no user type, so the screen cannot see it); change `MAIN_MENU_TARGETS` so one row becomes `'A'`; drop the case | All ten `CDEMO-MENU-OPTIONS` rows are USRTYPE `'U'` and `MenuOption` has no user-type member, so the screen's client-side gate is defensive dead code exactly as the legacy program's is with the shipped table (option 8's `(Admin Only)` label is commented out in the copybook). The refusal a user can actually observe comes from the api-gateway, where `MenuController` throws `CardDemoException(MSG_NO_ACCESS)` — answered `400` by `GlobalExceptionHandler` — and reaches line 23 verbatim through `useApi`. Changing the table to make the branch reachable would contradict the copybook | The client-side branch itself stays uncovered; it becomes reachable only if a future payload carries an admin-only row `[app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy; app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L136-L143; api-gateway MenuController]` |
| Fixture option labels are padded to the copybook `PIC X(35)` width | Use trimmed labels for readability | `MenuOptions.MAIN_MENU_OPTIONS` is transmitted untrimmed, so the padded form is the real wire shape the screen receives, and the rendered line is asserted exactly as the DOM presents it (`1. Account View`) | A reader may mistake the padding for stray whitespace `[app/cpy/COMEN02Y.cpy; carddemo-common MenuOptions]` |
| Line-23 assertions compare the raw `textContent` instead of `toHaveTextContent` | Use the jest-dom matcher everywhere for consistency with `Layout.test.tsx` | The admin-only literal ends in a blank that Testing Library's whitespace normalizer trims, so the matcher would leave the final character of a frozen literal unverified | The comparison fails on any wording change, which is the intent for a frozen literal `[app/cbl/COMEN01C.cbl:L140-L141]` |
| The suite adds NO observability surface of its own and reuses the delivered one: the SPA's tracing seam stays the `X-Correlation-Id` header the axios client sets, and the metrics, health / readiness and dashboard surfaces stay the Actuator, Prometheus and Grafana deliverables of Sections 6 and 9 | Assert on log output or spans from the suite; add a test-only logger; have the suite hit `/actuator/prometheus` | A colocated Jest suite emits no runtime telemetry, and the client that carries the correlation id is deliberately mocked here so no request leaves the test — instrumenting the suite would measure the test harness rather than the application. The delivered surface was re-verified live instead: Prometheus `/-/healthy` and Grafana `/api/health` both answer `200`, the api-gateway `/actuator/health` answers `UP` with its `liveness` / `readiness` groups, and `/actuator/prometheus` answers `401` for an unauthenticated caller with a structured body carrying a `traceId` | The screen's own request path is exercised only against the mock, so correlation-id propagation from this screen is covered by the client's suite and the live checks, not here `[frontend/src/api/client.ts:L41; observability/prometheus.yml]` |
| `frontend/src/main.tsx` and `frontend/src/App.tsx` are not authored by this suite | Author the entry and the 17-route table here so `npm run build` completes | Neither file is a dependency of this suite and neither is enumerated in the target layout (AAP 0.3.2 lists `pages/`, `components/`, `api/`, `hooks/`, `types/`); authoring them would invent a route-table contract every other screen tranche binds to. The module's own gates — `tsc --noEmit` and the full Jest suite — are green | Both files are now delivered — the Vite entry point and the role-gated route table (§35) — and `npm run build` completes, so the entry gap this row recorded is closed `[frontend/index.html; frontend/src/main.tsx; frontend/src/App.tsx]` |
## 23. Admin-Menu Screen Test Suite (`AdminMenuPage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The `../api` module is replaced with `jest.unstable_mockModule('../api', …)` rather than the `jest.mock('../api')` call the file contract names literally | Reproduce `jest.mock` verbatim and accept whatever it does; convert this one suite to the CJS transform so `jest.mock` hoisting applies; drop the mock and let the suite reach the real axios client | This project runs Jest 30 in NATIVE ESM (`"type": "module"`, preset `ts-jest/presets/default-esm`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `transform` with `useESM`), where Jest's module loader consults ONLY the registry populated by `unstable_mockModule`. A probe suite proved `jest.mock('../api', factory)` is silently INERT here — the real `getAdminMenu` loaded with `isMock === false` and no error was raised — so the literal form would leave the production axios client wired into all fourteen assertions while appearing to pass, and the contract's own mandate that `getAdminMenu` be served from a `MenuResponseDto` fixture could not hold. Switching this file to the CJS transform was rejected because it would fork the module's test infrastructure for one suite; the two sibling suites that already mock a module (`frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts`) use `unstable_mockModule` identically, so this reuses an established repository pattern. The mocked specifier (`../api`), the mocked symbol (`getAdminMenu` → `MenuResponseDto`) and the `ApiError` passthrough are all honoured exactly as written | `unstable_mockModule` is an experimental Jest API and its name may change in a future major; the suite also depends on `@jest/globals`, already part of the installed Jest distribution `[frontend/package.json jest.preset; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts:L34]` |
| The mocked `../api` barrel exposes `signon` in addition to `getAdminMenu` and `ApiError` | Return only the two symbols the contract names; pass the real `signon` through from `../api/auth` | An ES module that imports a name absent from the mocked namespace fails at LINK time, and `frontend/src/hooks/useSession.ts` statically imports `signon` from `../api`; the shell (`Layout`) pulls `useSession` in, so omitting `signon` aborts the whole suite before its first assertion. A `jest.fn()` was chosen over the real function so no code path can reach axios | One symbol in the mock surface beyond the contract's wording; it is never invoked, since no test signs in `[frontend/src/hooks/useSession.ts:L26]` |
| `ApiError` is passed through by importing it statically from the `../api` barrel, not from `../api/client` | Load it inside the factory with `await import('../api/client')`; obtain it with `jest.requireActual('../api')` | The barrel is the module this file's contract declares as its dependency, and the statically imported binding is the same class object `useApi` narrows against with `instanceof`, so identity is preserved and the factory stays synchronous. `jest.requireActual('../api')` CANNOT be used: `frontend/src/api/config.ts` reads `import.meta.env`, which is invalid on the CommonJS require path | The real barrel is evaluated once at link time, creating the axios instance without issuing a request — the same trade-off the existing `frontend/src/hooks/useApi.test.ts` already accepts `[frontend/src/api/config.ts:L8; frontend/src/hooks/useApi.test.ts:L21]` |
| `AdminMenuPage.test.tsx` leaves the router entirely REAL and observes navigation through a `LocationProbe` element that publishes `useLocation().pathname` into the DOM; `jest.unstable_mockModule` replaces only `../api` | (a) Partially mock the router with a synchronous `jest.requireActual` factory overriding `useNavigate` and assert on a navigate spy; (b) spread an `await import(...)` of the very specifier being mocked inside the factory | A `useNavigate` spy asserts that navigation was REQUESTED, not that it resolved: it replaces the router's own resolution, so a wrong or unregistered path still passes. Observing the resolved location asserts the transfer actually happened, which is what the legacy `XCTL` did. Mocking the router was also probed and rejected on mechanics: a factory that dynamically imports the specifier it is mocking recurses indefinitely and aborted the run with a V8 `Reached heap limit` fatal error, and `requireActual` is not available under native ESM. Keeping the module real avoids the whole class of problem, and `MemoryRouter` then comes from the same namespace as the hooks so the router's React context stays internally consistent | The probe observes the pathname only, so route-ELEMENT wiring is verified by the `App.tsx` route-table tests instead of here. This applies to every page suite that asserts a transfer `[frontend/src/pages/AdminMenuPage.test.tsx:L203-L211]`. Note that `react-router-dom` no longer exists in this project at all — it was replaced by `react-router` 8.3.0 (section 19) — so no row may describe mocking that specifier |
| The page is mounted as `<MemoryRouter><Layout><AdminMenuPage /></Layout></MemoryRouter>` rather than on its own | Render the page bare and assert on the chrome object it publishes; substitute a stub screen-chrome provider | `useScreenChrome()` returns an INERT detached context outside `Layout`, whose `setChrome` is a no-op, so a bare page renders neither the line-23 message region nor the line-24 key legend — and the contract's verbatim invalid-option message and its `ENTER=Continue` / `F3=Exit` scenarios both live in the shell. Mounting the real shell asserts what a user sees and matches how `App.tsx` composes the screen | The suite fails if the shared shell regresses, which is intended coupling for a workflow test `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx:L85-L106]` |
| The `Admin Menu` heading assertion is pinned to a heading LEVEL as well as a name — level 2, the level the row-4 screen heading actually renders at | Query by accessible name alone; query the element by CSS class or by a new test id | The shell's `Header` already contributes two headings of its own (`title01` `AWS Mainframe Modernization` as `<h1>`, `title02` `CardDemo` as `<h2>`), so the page's row-4 heading lives inside an established document outline. Asserting the level with the name pins that outline: the test fails if the screen heading is ever demoted or promoted, which a name-only query would not notice. It also needs no test id on the component under test | If the page's heading level changes the assertion must follow; no production code was touched to accommodate the test `[frontend/src/components/Header.tsx:L165,L176; frontend/src/pages/AdminMenuPage.tsx:L201; frontend/src/pages/AdminMenuPage.test.tsx:L56,L297]` |
| All FOUR admin options are asserted against the navigate spy (`/users`, `/users/add`, `/users/update`, `/users/delete`) | Assert only the two the contract requires as a minimum ("List Users + one more") | `CDEMO-ADMIN-OPT-COUNT` is 4 and the contract enumerates all four target routes, so covering every enumerated member closes the option-dispatch surface instead of sampling it | Four extra parameterised cases, ~0.1 s of runtime `[app/cpy/COADM02Y.cpy:L20-L42; app/cbl/COADM01C.cbl:L138-L145]` |
| Option names in the `MenuResponseDto` fixture keep the legacy `PIC X(35)` trailing padding (`'User List (Security)               '`), while row assertions compare the whitespace-normalized text | Trim the fixture names for readability; assert the raw padded `textContent` | The gateway serializes the 35-character space-padded legacy name verbatim, so a padded fixture is what the screen actually receives; Testing Library normalizes element text before comparison, so the assertion reads the collapsed label without weakening the fixture | A future gateway change to trim names would not be caught here; the frozen padding is asserted on the backend instead `[app/cpy/COADM02Y.cpy:L24-L42; api-gateway/src/main/java/com/carddemo/gateway/controller/MenuController.java:L418]` |

## 24. Account-View Screen Test Suite (`AccountViewPage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The suite registers its `../api` double with `jest.unstable_mockModule` and imports the page, the `Layout` shell and the hooks barrel dynamically in `beforeAll`, instead of the literal `jest.mock('../api')` named in the file's own build instruction | Reproduce `jest.mock('../api')` verbatim and accept a suite that silently drives the REAL client; move this one suite onto a CJS transform; obtain the real `ApiError` inside the factory with `jest.requireActual` | Under `npm test` the project runs Jest on the native-ESM runtime (`NODE_OPTIONS=--experimental-vm-modules`, `"type": "module"`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, ts-jest `useESM`), where static imports are linked before the module body executes, so `jest.mock` is never hoisted above them. A probe confirmed the imported `getAccount` remained the real function (`_isMockFunction === undefined`) with the factory in place, which would send the suite through axios and `import.meta` — the two things the file's instruction forbids. `jest.unstable_mockModule` is the ESM-correct equivalent and is already the established pattern of `frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts` in the same module; a CJS transform makes every `import.meta` in the graph a SyntaxError, and `requireActual` cannot load an ESM module. The specifier `'../api'` is reproduced character-for-character, and the factory publishes `getAccount`, `signon` and `ApiError` together because ESM linking fails on any named export an importing module requests that the mock omits | Test-infrastructure only, never bundled; if Jest stabilizes the API the call is renamed with no behavioural change `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts:L34; frontend/src/hooks/useApi.ts:L24]` |
| The page under test is rendered inside the real shared `Layout` shell, one import beyond the file's declared dependency list | Render the bare page and assert only its body fields; hand-roll a fake `useScreenChrome` by mocking `../components/Layout`; widen `Layout` to export its private `ScreenChromeContext` so a test can provide it | `useScreenChrome()` falls back to an inert `DETACHED_CONTEXT` whose `setChrome` is a no-op, so a bare page renders NEITHER the line-23 message region nor the line-24 key bar, leaving two required assertions unobservable — the verbatim `Account number must be a non zero 11 digit number` filter edit and the single `F3=Exit` key. `Layout` is the codebase's only provider of that chrome, is itself an AAP §0.4.3 deliverable, and `Layout.test.tsx` already renders a chrome-publishing page inside it, so the shell is reused rather than simulated. A hand-rolled fake would assert against a contract the test itself invented; widening `Layout`'s public API to serve a test was rejected as production-code churn | The suite now also fails if `Layout` regresses — intended integration coverage of the shell/page contract `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx:L85-L106; frontend/src/components/Layout.test.tsx:L15-L28,L47-L52]` |
| Caption assertions reproduce the BMS `INITIAL=` literals with their INTERNAL padding intact but without the trailing blanks of `'Middle Name: '`, `'Last Name : '`, `'State '`, `'City '`, `'EFT Account Id: '` and `'Government Issued Id Ref    : '` | Assert the trailing blank as well and fail against the rendered DOM; change `AccountViewPage.tsx` to emit the trailing blank; drop the padding question entirely by matching with the default whitespace-collapsing normalizer | The captions are asserted against what the frozen `AccountViewPage` renders, and it emits each BMS literal minus its trailing blank. A trailing space is insignificant in HTML text and carries no 3270 column meaning, whereas the internal padding IS the column alignment: it is asserted literally through `getDefaultNormalizer({ trim: false, collapseWhitespace: false })`, and negative assertions prove the collapsed forms (`Credit Limit :`, `Cash credit Limit :`, `Current Balance :`, `Government Issued Id Ref :`) are absent. Editing the page to emit trailing blanks would change a frozen component to suit a test | A page edit that trims internal padding still fails the suite; only insignificant trailing blanks go unasserted `[app/bms/COACTVW.bms:L116,L191,L246,L250,L276,L300,L325,L341; frontend/src/pages/AccountViewPage.tsx:L209-L244]` |
## 25. Card-Detail Screen Test Suite (`CardDetailPage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

Decisions taken while building `frontend/src/pages/CardDetailPage.test.tsx`, the Jest + React
Testing Library suite for the card-detail workflow (`COCRDSL` / `CCDL` / `COCRDSLC`). Each row is a
choice a competent engineer could reasonably have made differently, or a departure from a literal
reading of the file's own instruction, so it is recorded here rather than in a code comment, per the
Explainability rule (AAP §0.7.2).

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The `../api` module is replaced with `jest.unstable_mockModule('../api', …)` plus `await import(…)` in `beforeAll`, NOT with the `jest.mock('../api')` the file's instruction names | Reproduce `jest.mock('../api')` literally and accept whatever it does; keep `jest.mock` as dead code alongside the working registration; abandon native ESM and move this suite to the CJS transform | The instruction's two mandates — `getCard` must be a spy returning a `CardDetailResponseDto`, and `import.meta` must never be evaluated — are BOTH unreachable through `jest.mock` in this project. A throwaway probe (`src/pages/blitzy_adhoc_test_probe.test.tsx`, since deleted) that called `jest.mock('../api', factory)` and then read the statically imported `getCard` printed `JEST_MOCK_INTERCEPTED=false`: under native ESM (`"type": "module"`, preset `ts-jest/presets/default-esm`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `--experimental-vm-modules`) every static import is linked and evaluated before the module body runs, so the registration arrives too late, the REAL `../api` is loaded, and with it `src/api/config.ts:L8` `import.meta.env`. `unstable_mockModule` is Jest's own ESM equivalent and is already the established pattern of the sibling suite `frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts`. The module specifier `'../api'`, the mocked member `getCard`, and the passed-through `ApiError` are all reproduced verbatim; only the registration API differs. Dead `jest.mock` scaffolding was rejected because unreachable code is itself a contract violation | Ties the suite to an `unstable_`-prefixed API that Jest may rename; the two sibling suites already carry the same exposure, so a rename is one mechanical change across the module. Verified: coverage of `src/api/config.ts` and `src/api/client.ts` under this suite is 0 % of statements, proving neither module body — hence no `import.meta` — ever executed `[frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts; frontend/package.json; frontend/src/api/config.ts]` |
| The page is rendered as `<Layout><CardDetailPage /></Layout>`, adding `frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx` to the suite's imports even though it is not one of the four declared dependencies | Render the page bare and assert only what its own body emits; export the chrome context provider separately so a test can supply it; stub `useScreenChrome` | `CardDetailPage` publishes its line-23 message and its line-24 legend into the shell through `useScreenChrome`, and outside a `Layout` that hook returns the inert `DETACHED_CONTEXT` whose `setChrome` is a no-op (§16). The page also renders NO submit control of its own — the legend's `ENTER=Search Cards` key is the only submit affordance (§16). So rendered bare, BOTH mandated scenarios — the two verbatim edit messages and the `ENTER=Search Cards` / `F3=Exit` handlers — are unobservable, and the suite could not reach its required outcome. `Layout.tsx` is an existing on-disk AAP §0.4.3 target and already a direct dependency of the page under test, so nothing was invented. Exporting a bare provider was rejected because it would widen the shell's public API purely for a test; stubbing the hook was rejected because it would stop exercising the real publish path | The suite now fails if the shell regresses, not only if the page does — which is the intended coupling for a screen whose message and key regions live in the shell. `Layout.test.tsx` covers the shell independently `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx; frontend/src/pages/CardDetailPage.tsx]` |
| `ApiError` is passed through the mock factory as a class declared IN the suite with the real four-parameter constructor `(status, message, body?, isOptimisticLockConflict = false)`, not re-exported from `../api/client` | Import the real class from `../api` (as `useApi.test.ts` does); auto-mock it; drop it from the factory | `useApi` uses `ApiError` as a runtime value on both branches — `err instanceof ApiError` and `new ApiError(0, …)` — so an auto-mocked or absent export would throw on the failure path and break ES-module linking. Re-exporting the real class is the obvious choice and was rejected for exactly one reason: `client.ts` statically imports `./config` and calls `getApiBaseUrl()` at module scope, so loading it evaluates `import.meta.env`, which this file's instruction forbids. The redeclared class mirrors the real constructor argument-for-argument, and `useSession.test.ts` sets the precedent. The suite proves the passthrough works rather than asserting it structurally: a rejected plain `Error` reaches the screen as text only because `useApi` successfully constructed a real `ApiError` from the mocked module | The local class and `frontend/src/api/client.ts` must stay in step; a constructor change there is caught immediately, because the suite's own `new ApiError(404, …)` is type-checked against `ApiErrorResponse` from `../types` `[frontend/src/api/client.ts; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts]` |
| PF3 navigation is observed through a sibling `<Route path="/cards" …>` rendering a probe element inside the same `<Routes>`, instead of spying on `useNavigate` | Mock `react-router-dom` and assert `navigate` was called with `/cards`; assert on the `MemoryRouter` history object | The requirement is that the `F3=Exit` handler is WIRED, which a real routed transition demonstrates end to end — router configuration included — whereas a `useNavigate` spy proves only that a function was called. It also keeps `react-router-dom` unmocked, so the `useParams` read that the load-by-parameter scenario depends on stays real | Adds one route to the harness; it renders nothing in the card-detail cases and cannot mask a failure, since a missing transition leaves the probe absent `[frontend/src/pages/CardDetailPage.tsx:L86]` |
| The factory also exports `signon: jest.fn()`, which the suite never calls | Export only `getCard` and `ApiError`; import `../hooks/useApi` directly instead of the `../hooks` barrel to keep `useSession` out of the graph | `Layout` reads `useSession`, and `../testing/sessionHarness` — the test-only module that seeds the session (§37) — reaches that same module through the `../hooks` barrel, which binds `signon` from `../api`. ES modules resolve named imports at LINK time, so a factory missing `signon` fails the whole suite with a "does not provide an export" error before any test runs. Reaching past the `../hooks` barrel was rejected because the barrel is the declared dependency | A future `../api` import added to the loaded graph must be added to the factory too; the failure is a loud link error, never a silent pass `[frontend/src/hooks/useSession.ts:L26; frontend/src/hooks/index.ts]` |
| Card fixtures are rows 1-2 of the repository's own `app/data/ASCII/carddata.txt` / `cardxref.txt` (`0500024453765740` / `Aniya Von` / `2023-03-09`, `0683586198171516` / `Ward Jones` / `2025-07-13`) | Invent readable placeholder values; use a well-known industry test PAN | The seed is the same Apache-licensed data the Flyway seed migrations load, so the suite asserts against values the running system actually serves, and no card number is introduced that could resemble a live credential. A widely published test PAN was rejected for that second reason alone | Fixture drift if the seed files change; they are frozen legacy inputs `[app/data/ASCII/carddata.txt; app/data/ASCII/cardxref.txt]` |
| Caption assertions pass an identity `normalizer` to `getByText` so the BMS column padding is matched exactly (`Name on card      :`, not `Name on card :`) | Use Testing Library's default normalizer; assert with a regular expression; assert `textContent` on a queried node | The captions are frozen `DFHMDF INITIAL` literals whose internal spacing IS the 3270 column layout, and the default normalizer collapses runs of whitespace, so a caption silently re-spaced would still pass. The identity normalizer makes the padding part of the contract — confirmed by mutation: collapsing one caption's padding fails the suite | Slightly stricter than the rest of the module's queries; intentional for literals the mapset freezes `[app/bms/COCRDSL.bms:L103-L125]` |

## 26. Card-Update Screen Test Suite (`CardUpdatePage.test.tsx` checkpoint)

Decisions taken while delivering the colocated Jest / React Testing Library suite for the
card-update screen (`COCRDUP` / CICS `CCUP` / `app/cbl/COCRDUPC.cbl`).

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The suite registers the `../api` module mock with `jest.unstable_mockModule` and imports `./CardUpdatePage`, `../components/Layout` and `../hooks` dynamically in `beforeAll`, instead of the `jest.mock('../api')` form named in the file's own build instruction | Reproduce `jest.mock('../api')` verbatim and accept that the page binds the real client; move this one module onto the CJS transform; inject `getCard` / `updateCard` as props so no mock is needed | The module runs Jest 30 in NATIVE ESM (`"type": "module"`, preset `ts-jest/presets/default-esm`, `extensionsToTreatAsEsm`, `transform` with `useESM`), where a test file's static imports are evaluated BEFORE any statement in its body — so a `jest.mock` call cannot intercept them and the page would load the real `api/client`, construct the axios instance and evaluate `import.meta.env` through `getApiBaseUrl()`, the exact evaluation the same instruction forbids. `unstable_mockModule` plus a dynamic import after registration is the only interception point in that runtime, and it is already the established pattern in this module (`frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts`). No module reset is used, so the page shares one React instance with Testing Library. Every other literal of the instruction — messages, field widths, key labels, `cardExpiraionDate` — is reproduced byte-for-byte | The three dynamic imports must stay inside `beforeAll`: promoting any of them to a static import silently re-binds the real client and the suite would exercise axios. A future switch to the CJS transform would make the `unstable_` API unnecessary `[frontend/package.json; frontend/src/hooks/useSession.test.ts:L34]` |
| `ApiError` is "passed through" as a locally declared class carrying the client's exact public surface (`status`, `body`, `isOptimisticLockConflict`, `name === 'ApiError'`), exported from the mock factory | Re-export the real class from `../api/client` inside an async factory; omit the export and assert only on the rendered message; reject with a bare `Error` carrying a `status` property | The page branches on `error instanceof ApiError` and on `error.isOptimisticLockConflict`, and `useApi` re-wraps a non-`ApiError` as `new ApiError(0, message)`, so the mocked export must be a constructible class IDENTICAL to the binding the page holds — a mock function or a bare `Error` makes the 409 branch unreachable and would silently test the generic-failure path instead. Reaching the real class means importing `api/client`, which instantiates axios and reads `import.meta` at module scope | The stand-in must track the real class's four constructor parameters and three members; a change to that shape is caught by the client's own consumers rather than by this suite `[frontend/src/api/client.ts:L74-L91; frontend/src/hooks/useApi.ts]` |
| Every case renders the page inside the REAL `Layout` on `/cards/update`, with sibling `/cards/view` and `/cards` routes and a `useLocation` probe | Render the page bare; mock `../components/Layout` and assert on the captured chrome object; mock `useNavigate` and assert on the spy | `useScreenChrome()` outside a `Layout` is deliberately inert (§13), so a bare render DISCARDS the line-23 message and the line-24 legend — precisely the outputs the screen contract freezes (`Changes validated.Press F5 to save`, `ENTER=Process` / `F3=Exit` / `F5=Save` / `F12=Cancel`). Asserting through the real `ErrorBanner` and `PFKeyBar` additionally covers the physical AID-key path — the legend withholds `F5=Save` and `F12=Cancel` until ENTER validates the pending changes, while the shell still dispatches the darkened AID from the keyboard — and the `role="alert"` / `role="status"` split that separates an error from an informational outcome, and it is what makes `seedSignedOnSession(user, 'U')` seeding observable at all. The routes are the delivered ones: the card number and account id travel in the router location state, never in a path segment, so the update screen is `/cards/update` and its F12 target is `/cards/view` | The suite fails if the shell's regions are restructured, which is intended — the page publishes into them `[frontend/src/components/Layout.tsx; frontend/src/pages/cardSelection.ts]` |
