| A new `POST /transactions/key` re-expresses `COTRN02C VALIDATE-INPUT-KEY-FIELDS` on its own, and the add screen's ENTER turn runs key edits → this read → the eleven blank guards → the `CONFIRMI` evaluation | Move the client's blank guards after the add call and let the add itself report the two literals; resolve the keys inside the add request; accept the two literals as unreachable | `PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` performs `VALIDATE-INPUT-KEY-FIELDS` and only THEN `VALIDATE-INPUT-DATA-FIELDS`, and the key paragraph is an `EVALUATE` whose supplied-account branch performs `READ-CXACAIX-FILE` and `MOVE XREF-CARD-NUM TO CARDNINI`, its supplied-card branch `READ-CCXREF-FILE` and `MOVE XREF-ACCT-ID TO ACTIDINI`. The cross-reference READ therefore happens BEFORE the eleven blank guards — which is precisely why `Account ID NOT found...` and `Card Number NOT found...` were unreachable in the SPA, whose client-side blank guards ran first and always won. Deferring the guards instead would have inverted a different pair of literals; resolving inside the add would not run at all until every data field was filled. The paragraph is also the first thing `COPY-LAST-TRAN-DATA` performs, so one endpoint serves both entry points. The keys travel in the BODY because one of them is a card number (§40.4) | One extra round trip per ENTER turn on the add screen. Verified at runtime: an account of `99999999999` with every other field blank now returns 404 and publishes `Account ID NOT found...`, a card of `9999999999999999` publishes `Card Number NOT found...`, both keys blank issues ZERO requests and publishes `Account or Card Number must be entered...`, and a resolvable account fills `Card #` then advances to the first data-field guard `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L166-L232, L469-L520]` |
| `F5=Copy Last Tran.` performs the key resolution FIRST, then copies the eleven data fields, then runs the ENTER turn | Copy first and resolve afterwards; copy the key fields from the last transaction too | `COPY-LAST-TRAN-DATA` is literally those three steps in that order, and the eleven MOVEs it performs are `TTYPCD` through `MZIP` — the DATA fields. The key fields are owned by the resolution step, not the copy, which is why the card that lands in `CARDNIN` is the one belonging to the account the operator typed and not the last transaction's card. Copying the keys would silently retarget the entry | The two key fields and the eleven data fields come from different records, which is what the source does. Verified at runtime: F5 with only an account typed issues key → last → key, fills the card from the cross-reference and the eleven fields from transaction `0000001996722787` `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L469-L520]` |

### 41.3 A rejected field is marked, not reddened

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| On all three transaction screens a rejected control carries `aria-invalid` and the cursor, and NO red frame and NO `*` marker | Apply the `.fieldError` treatment built for the card and account screens, for consistency across the app | A census of every online program settles it: `COTRN00C`, `COTRN01C` and `COTRN02C` contain ZERO occurrences of `DFHRED` and ZERO of `MOVE '*'`. Only four programs ever recolour a data field — `COCRDUPC` (12), `COCRDSLC` (4), `COACTUPC` (3) and `COACTVWC` (2) — and they are exactly the four screens where the treatment IS delivered. `COUSR02C`'s single `DFHRED` moves into `ERRMSGC`, the message line, not a field. On the transaction screens the legacy marks a rejected field ONLY by placing the cursor on it and publishing its line-23 message, so a red box here would be observable output the program does not produce, which AAP §0.7.2 Rule 5 forbids. `aria-invalid` is not visible output and carries no BMS analogue to contradict; it is what WCAG 4.1.2 requires of a rejected control, and it conflicts with nothing the mapset declares | An assistive-technology user is told which control failed while a sighted user reads the message and follows the cursor — the same asymmetry the terminal had. Pinned by two tests (the list screen's selection control and a document-wide `fieldError` sweep on the add screen) and measured in the browser: with a rejection standing, zero `fieldError` elements exist and all fourteen inputs frame in the normal `rgb(31,156,64)` `[app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl; app/cbl/COTRN01C.cbl; app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl; AAP §0.7.2]` |
| Every message these three screens publish reaches line 23 through the RED `role="alert"` channel, including the `Confirm to add this transaction...` prompt; only the successful-add line uses the GREEN `role="status"` channel | Route prompts and boundary notices through the polite channel as an accessibility improvement | The colour is in the source. `COTRN00C` and `COTRN01C` contain no `MOVE DFHGREEN` at all, so every message those two screens send is the map's declared `ERRMSG COLOR=RED`. `COTRN02C` contains exactly ONE, on the successful-write branch (`MOVE DFHGREEN TO ERRMSGC` before the `Transaction added successfully.` string) — and that one message is the only one the SPA routes to the green channel. Making the prompts polite would recolour them, since §16's rule keys the colour off the `role="status"` branch. Every message that does reach the assertive region is an error, a refused action or a prompt demanding the operator act, so nothing passive is announced assertively | None identified. The `id="screenMessageLine"` anchor is carried by BOTH text-bearing variants, so an `aria-describedby` set on a faulted control never dangles when the outcome turns informational `[app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L726-L731; app/bms/COTRN02.bms]` |

### 41.4 The list table is laid out from the mapset's own column rules

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `/transactions` uses `table-layout: fixed` with a `<colgroup>` derived from the row-9 hyphen runs, plus a table `min-width` equal to their sum | Leave the content-driven auto layout; set the widths in the stylesheet; give the last column an explicit width too | The BMS rule runs ARE the column widths — `COTRN00` row 9 paints 3 / 16 / 8 / 26 / 12 hyphens at columns 2 / 8 / 27 / 38 / 67 — so declaring them is transcription, not styling, and it fixes the reported header drift of up to +83.67px as rows arrived, changed or emptied. A 3270 column never moves. The last column is deliberately left width-less so a container wider than the floor hands it the slack and the right-aligned amount stays against the frame's right edge, where the mapset puts it. The `min-width` exists because that slack can go NEGATIVE: browser measurement of the first attempt found the Amount column allocated 0px at 375 and 320 CSS px, its cells clipped to nothing and — because a zero-width column contributes nothing to a scroll range — unreachable even at maximum container scroll. Holding the table to the summed width puts the shortfall into the wrapper's scroll range instead | The table can now exceed its container, which `div.tableScroll` absorbs so the page itself never scrolls sideways. Verified at four widths: column shift 0.000px across the populated, filtered and empty states; Amount 101px wide with 43.219px of painted ink at both 320 and 375, fully revealed at maximum scroll (234px / 179px) including by ArrowRight keypresses on the focusable region; and at 1440 the Amount right edge is 0.000px from the container edge, i.e. the floor is inert there `[app/bms/COTRN00.bms:L120-L150]` |
| An empty browse renders NO row, and a page indicator of `0` on an empty result is FAITHFUL | Paint a "nothing found" placeholder row; force the indicator to 1 | The invented literal `No transactions to display` is removed: `COTRN00C` paints no such text — it leaves the ten row fields at LOW-VALUES and publishes any message on line 23. A placeholder row inside the body also picks up the row hover treatment, which belongs to selectable rows alone. The `0` is the program's own arithmetic: `PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` does `MOVE 0 TO CDEMO-CT00-PAGE-NUM` and then `PROCESS-PAGE-FORWARD`, which increments only when rows were read, so an empty result leaves the field at zero and `MOVE CDEMO-CT00-PAGE-NUM TO PAGENUMI` sends it | An operator sees `Page: 0` beside an empty table, as the terminal showed. Verified: the string is absent from `innerText`, `outerHTML` and the shipped bundle, and the service's own `message` is `null`, so nothing could supply it `[app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl:L222-L226; app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl PROCESS-PAGE-FORWARD]` |
| A browse that FAILS leaves the rows and the page indicator already on screen in place | Clear the table and reset the indicator, as before | Both paging-boundary branches of `COTRN00C` carry `SET SEND-ERASE-NO TO TRUE`, so the map is re-sent WITHOUT erasing: the rows already painted, and the page number beside them, stay on screen while line 23 carries the message. The service reproduces this for a boundary by returning the message together with the full current page; a transport failure returns nothing at all, so the client holds the last page it successfully received. The reported defect was an offline PF8 wiping the table and silently resetting the indicator from 3 to 1, discarding the operator's browse position on a message that never claimed the position had changed | The displayed page can be one turn stale after a failure. The message belongs to the current turn alone and is never taken from the held page, so a stale success line cannot appear beside a fresh failure. Verified under offline emulation with 6,493 samples: rows stayed at 10 and the indicator at `3` in every one, all four function keys were disabled together with the `X SYSTEM` input-inhibit indicator, and the message was a BMS-style line rather than the raw transport string `[app/cbl/COTRN00C.cbl PROCESS-PF7-KEY / PROCESS-PF8-KEY]` |

### 41.5 Fields the error path does not write are BLANK, and one caption wraps

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| A blank-id ENTER on `/transactions/view` clears all thirteen display fields | Leave the previous record on screen; grey it out | `COTRN01.bms` declares every one of the thirteen `ATTRB=(ASKIP,NORM)` with NO `FSET`, so their modified-data tags are off and `RECEIVE MAP` returns them as LOW-VALUES; `PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` then moves no value into them, because both the clearing MOVE and the read are guarded by `IF NOT ERR-FLG-ON`; and `SEND ... ERASE` paints an empty field for each. Keeping the previous transaction's values beside a fresh error attributes them to an id nobody entered | None. Verified: all thirteen `<dd>` are empty and none of the previous record's four distinctive values survives anywhere in the document, with zero API requests issued `[app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L60-L200; app/cbl/COTRN01C.cbl PROCESS-ENTER-KEY]` |
| The `/transactions/add` confirmation caption is allowed to WRAP (`white-space: pre-wrap`) | Shrink the font at narrow widths; truncate with an ellipsis; abbreviate the literal | The literal is `LENGTH=55 COLOR=TURQUOISE POS=(21,6)` and is frozen, but a 320px viewport holds about 42 character cells, so it cannot fit on one line there — it was measured losing `confirm :` at 375 and everything before `e confirm :` at 320. Wrapping keeps every one of the 55 characters legible; truncating or abbreviating would withhold or alter frozen output. `pre-wrap` rather than `normal` because the caption's internal spacing is significant | The caption occupies two rows below 48rem, so the character grid gains a row there. Verified at all three widths: the 55-character literal compares equal byte-for-byte, and a Range over its last nine characters lies inside both the viewport and the label's painted box at 1440, 375 and 320 `[app/bms/COTRN02.bms:L200-L215]` |
| Two numeric-overflow guards: a digit run wider than the 16-character key is padded as a STRING and never parsed, and an account id wider than `PIC 9(11)` is refused by the numeric edit | Catch `NumberFormatException` and map it to the not-found path; validate the width in the controller | `TRAN-ID` is `PIC X(16)` and `XREF-ACCT-ID` is `PIC 9(11)`, so a longer run is unenterable on the 3270 but can still arrive over the wire. Three call sites converted such a value with `Long.valueOf` / `Long.parseLong` and raised `NumberFormatException` past `Long.MAX_VALUE`, surfacing as an unhandled HTTP 500 with a generic body — measured on both the detail read and the list filter. A run that wide cannot equal any key of the declared width, so the correct outcome is the ordinary miss the legacy READ produces, and for the account id the numeric edit's own verbatim literal. Catching the exception would reach the same outcome by treating a predictable input as an error | None. Pinned by nine service tests across the three call sites and re-verified on the running stack: the detail read and the list filter both answer a 19-digit value without a 5xx `[app/cpy/CVTRA05Y.cpy; app/cpy/CVACT03Y.cpy]` |

## 42. QA Remediation — Bill-Payment and Report Screens (`COBIL00` / `CORPT00`)

Runtime QA of `/billpay` (CICS `CB00`, program `COBIL00C`) and `/reports` (CICS `CR00`, program `CORPT00C`) raised ten findings. Resolving them turned on two source facts established by census rather than by reading the findings: `COBIL00C` and `CORPT00C` each contain **zero** `MOVE DFHRED` and **zero** `MOVE '*'`, and each contains **exactly one** `MOVE DFHGREEN TO ERRMSGC`. Both programs therefore mark a rejected field by cursor and message alone, and both carry one message field whose colour changes on exactly one branch.

### 42.1 The two-channel message contract

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `BillPaymentResponseDto` gains `errorMessage` beside `message`, and `ReportResponseDto` gains `message` beside `errorMessage`, so the outcome — and with it the `ERRMSGC` colour — travels as the CHOICE OF MEMBER | Keep one member and have the client decide the colour by comparing the text against its own copy of the expected wording; add an explicit `messageColour` or `channel` enum field | `COBIL00` and `CORPT00` each declare ONE message field, `ERRMSG POS=(23,1) COLOR=RED`, and each program overrides its colour at exactly one site: `COBIL00C:L526` on the successful-payment branch and `CORPT00C` immediately before the submission acknowledgement. The colour is information the text does not carry, so it must reach the client somehow. It had been reaching it by text comparison — `ReportPage` composed `<name> report submitted for printing ...` locally and compared, so any wording drift on either side would have rendered a failure in success green (proved by a test that sends the acknowledgement wording on the error channel and asserts it still renders red). A dedicated colour field was rejected as inventing a wire concept the mapset does not have: the outcome already determines the colour, so naming the outcome is sufficient | At most one member is ever populated on either DTO, which every consumer must respect. Eleven `reporting-service` tests that asserted the acknowledgement on `getErrorMessage()` were moved to `getMessage()` `[app/bms/COBIL00.bms:L146-L151; app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L522-L533; app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl]` |
| On `BillPaymentResponseDto`, `errorMessage` carries ONLY the nothing-to-pay refusal; the confirm prompt and the payment acknowledgement both travel on `message` and are told apart by `transactionId` | Move the confirm prompt onto `errorMessage` too, so `message` means "green" literally | `COBIL00C` has THREE in-band outcomes, not two, and they differ in cursor target as well as colour: the refusal moves `-1` to `ACTIDINL` (L203), the confirm prompt moves it to `CONFIRML` (L239), and the acknowledgement re-cursors `ACTIDIN` through `INITIALIZE-ALL-FIELDS` (L524). Folding the prompt onto `errorMessage` would have made that member ambiguous and forced the client to re-derive which outcome it was from the balance — business logic on the client. `transactionId` is an exact discriminator because L526 (the sole `DFHGREEN`) and the `TRAN-ID` generation sit on the same branch | The invariant "a populated `message` WITH a transaction id is the GREEN send, WITHOUT one is a RED send" is documented on the member and pinned by a service test. Runtime census of all four `/billpay` outcomes confirmed exactly one `role="status"` + `rgb(51,255,102)` and three `role="alert"` + `rgb(255,59,48)` |

### 42.2 The zero balance reaches the screen with its refusal

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| A zero-or-negative balance answers HTTP **200** carrying BOTH `currentBalance` and `errorMessage`, instead of throwing | Keep throwing a business exception (4xx with no balance); return 200 with the message but no balance | `COBIL00C` moves `ACCT-CURR-BAL` into `CURBALI` at L193-194 **unconditionally**, and only then tests `IF ACCT-CURR-BAL <= ZEROS` at L198. The map send that carries `You have nothing to pay...` therefore carries the balance too. Refusing with an error status withheld the balance, which left the screen showing the PREVIOUS account's money beside this account's id — the reported defect | The screen now repaints the balance on every answered turn, so a client that ignored the body of a 4xx must read the 200 body instead `[app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L190-L206]` |
| On a FAILED read the displayed balance is deliberately LEFT in place | Blank the balance whenever the read misses | `COBIL0AO REDEFINES COBIL0AI`, and a byte walk of `app/cpy-bms/COBIL00.CPY` shows `ACTIDIN` at offset 169/11, `CURBAL` at 187/14, `CONFIRM` at 208/1 and `ERRMSG` at 216/78 with input and output sharing every offset; `CURBAL` is `ATTRB=(ASKIP,FSET,NORM)`, so the FSET tag returns the displayed value on `RECEIVE MAP` and the send echoes it back. `READ-ACCTDAT-FILE` sends the map from inside itself on `NOTFND`, i.e. BEFORE the L193 move, so the legacy shows the previous balance beside `Account ID NOT found...` as well | A not-found turn shows a balance belonging to the previous account id. That is what the mapset's FSET tag does; the reported defect was the case that DOES reach the account, which 42.2 above fixes `[app/cpy-bms/COBIL00.CPY; app/bms/COBIL00.bms:L103-L106]` |

### 42.3 The account key is exactly eleven ASCII digits

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `BillPaymentService.parseAccountId` accepts ONLY eleven ASCII digits and raises `Account ID NOT found...` for everything else; the `@Size(max = 11)` bean-validation constraint was REMOVED from `BillPaymentRequestDto.accountId` | Keep `Long.parseLong`; keep the width constraint and only change its message; add a numeric-format message of the kind other screens use | `Long.parseLong` accepted three classes of value that are not the stored key: an UNPADDED id (`1`, `01`, `0000000001`), which resolved to a real account and disclosed its balance; Unicode decimal digits (full-width U+FF10…, Arabic-Indic U+0660…), which `Character.digit` accepts, likewise resolving; and runs wide enough to overflow `long`. `ACCT-ID` is `PIC 9(11)`, `ACTIDIN` is `LENGTH=11` and the VSAM catalogue records `KEYLEN 11` — three independent confirmations of the width. `COBIL00C` performs NO numeric or width edit (only the blank test), so every value the operator can enter that is not a stored key has exactly ONE legacy outcome, and a format message would be invented output. The width constraint answered a subset of malformed values with a second, differently-worded 400, so it was removed rather than reworded | An over-width id now reaches the service rather than the request contract, which is pinned by a controller test. Twelve malformed value classes were verified at runtime to return a uniform 404 with the single literal `[app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L155-L170; app/bms/COBIL00.bms:L80-L90; app/catlg/LISTCAT.txt]` |
| That constraint's message, `'Account number must be a non zero 11 digit number'`, is not reused anywhere on this screen | Keep it as the width message | It is an 88-level (`SEARCHED-ACCT-ZEROES` / `SEARCHED-ACCT-NOT-NUMERIC`) declared at `COACTVWC:L125,L127` and `COACTUPC:L493,L495` and **SET by neither program** — the same unreachable-literal shape §40 already removed from the card feature. The reachable account-format literals are three different strings, one per program: `COACTVWC:L672` moves `'Account Filter must  be a non-zero 11 digit number'` (two spaces after `must`, hyphen in `non-zero`), `COACTUPC:L1806-1809` STRINGs `'Account Number if supplied must be a 11 digit Non-Zero Number'`, and `COTRN02C:L199` moves `'Account ID must be Numeric...'`. `COBIL00C` has none | The literal remains in use on the account and transaction-add surfaces, which are outside this checkpoint's screens; recorded there as owed work — **that work is now complete, see §44.7**, so the literal is served nowhere. `ErrorEnvelopeTest`'s probe was moved to a reachable literal so the suite no longer enshrines an unreachable one `[app/cbl/COACTVWC.cbl:L125-L127, L666-L676; app/cbl/COACTUPC.cbl:L493-L496, L1801-L1813; app/cbl/COTRN02C.cbl:L199]` |

### 42.4 Colour and width literals on `COBIL00`

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `Enter Acct ID:` is painted with a new `.green` utility rather than the shared turquoise `.prompt` class | Leave it on `.prompt` with every other entry caption | `COBIL00.bms:L80-L84` declares that caption `LENGTH=14 POS=(6,6) COLOR=GREEN`. It is the one entry caption in the seventeen mapsets that is not turquoise, so a single-purpose utility is the minimal way to say so | One more utility class in the shared stylesheet; verified at runtime as `rgb(51,255,102)` against the turquoise `rgb(0,204,204)` of the balance caption beside it |
| The balance caption keeps its trailing pad column: the literal is `'Your current balance is: '`, 25 characters | Trim to the 24 visible characters | `COBIL00.bms:L110-L114` declares `LENGTH=25` at `POS=(11,6)`, and `CURBAL` starts at column 32 — the pad column is what holds the gap. `OutputField` renders the pair inside `div.detailField`, whose `dt` already carries `white-space: pre`, so the column survives rendering once the JSX literal contains it. A `.billPay__row > dt` rule was written for this and then removed when a test proved it matched nothing | None; pinned by a test asserting length 25 and verified at runtime with the last character code 32 |
| The 53-character confirm prompt renders `white-space: pre-wrap` rather than `pre` | Keep `pre`; shorten the literal; allow horizontal scroll | `COBIL00.bms:L133-L137` declares `LENGTH=53` at `POS=(15,6)`; under `pre` the trailing `m: ` was pushed outside the viewport at 375px. `pre-wrap` preserves every interior space and the trailing pad while allowing the run to wrap, which is the only way a 53-column literal fits a 320px screen without losing characters | The prompt occupies two lines below 48rem. Verified with a DOM Range over the last six characters at 1440/375/320: fully inside both the label box and the viewport at all three, zero elements past the right edge `[app/bms/COBIL00.bms:L133-L137]` |
| The same money value renders as a bordered green box on `/accounts` and as plain blue text on `/billpay` | Converge the two on one treatment | This is what the two mapsets declare. `COACTVW.bms:L159-L163` gives `ACURBAL` `HILIGHT=UNDERLINE`, `JUSTIFY=(RIGHT)`, `PICOUT='+ZZZ,ZZZ,ZZZ.99'` and **no `COLOR`** — the default tone with an underline. `COBIL00.bms:L103-L106` gives `CURBAL` `COLOR=BLUE` and no `HILIGHT`. Converging them would override a declared attribute on one of the two screens | Cross-screen inconsistency is visible to a user comparing the two, and is intended `[app/bms/COACTVW.bms:L159-L163; app/bms/COBIL00.bms:L103-L106]` |

### 42.5 A rejected field is marked by cursor and flag, never by colour

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| Neither screen reddens a rejected entry field or writes a `*` marker into it; the marking is the cursor plus the line-23 message, with `aria-invalid` alongside | Apply the `.fieldError` treatment these screens' controls already accept, for consistency with `/cards/update` and `/accounts/update` | `COBIL00C` and `CORPT00C` each contain **zero** `MOVE DFHRED` and **zero** `MOVE '*'`. Every rejection in both programs is `MOVE <literal> TO WS-MESSAGE` plus `MOVE -1 TO <field>L` — twelve such sites in `CORPT00C` alone (L264/270/276/285/291/297 for the empty tests, L334/342/350/360/368/376 for the invalid tests, L403/423 for the two composite calendar edits). A red frame there is observable output the mapset never declares, which the spec-literal rule forbids. The screens that DO redden a field — `COCRDUPC`, `COCRDSLC`, `COACTUPC`, `COACTVWC` — are exactly the ones where `DFHRED` appears, and they keep the treatment | A sighted user distinguishes the offending one of six date boxes by the focus outline rather than by colour. `aria-invalid` is not visible output and has no BMS analogue to contradict, so it travels alone and assistive technology is still told which control was rejected. Pinned by a test on each screen asserting `aria-invalid="true"`, focus on the field, and a document-wide `.fieldError` count of zero |
| The confirmation field on each screen is described by its own `(Y/N)` literal | Leave the field with no description; describe it with the line-23 message only | The `(Y/N)` literal at `COBIL00.bms:L143` and its `CORPT00` counterpart exists to tell the operator what the one-character field accepts, which is the definition of an accessible description. `invalidFieldProps` already emitted it on `/billpay`; `/reports` was given a matching `#reportConfirmValues` id | None; both verified at runtime as resolving to a live element whose text is exactly `(Y/N)` |

### 42.6 `CORPT00`'s date components are never protected

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| All six date components stay enterable in every state of the screen; the `disabled` attribute outside the `Custom` window, and the dimmed treatment that went with it, were REMOVED (superseding the second half of the §19 row) | Keep them disabled and merely raise the dimmed colour's contrast; keep them disabled and add a non-colour cue | `CORPT00.bms:L127-L193` declares every one of the six `ATTRB=(FSET,NORM,NUM,UNPROT) COLOR=GREEN HILIGHT=UNDERLINE`, and `CORPT00C` moves NO attribute byte to ANY field — it contains `COPY DFHBMSCA` at L149 and not one `MOVE` to a `…C OF CORPT0A` member. The mapset therefore offers one entry treatment and never protects these boxes. The window selection decides only whether the entered range is READ: `CORPT00C:L212 EVALUATE TRUE` matches the selectors in order and inspects the date parts under the `CUSTOM` branch alone. Removing the disabling is strictly more faithful AND removes both consequences at once — the hue-only distinction measured at 2.80:1, and this screen being the only one with three input treatments | A range typed against `Monthly` or `Yearly` is now silently ignored rather than being unenterable. That is what the legacy does, and it is what `buildReportRequest` implements: it sends the parts for `CUSTOM` only, verified at the wire (`{"yearly":"Y","confirm":"Y"}` with the typed parts absent) and in the service log (the derived range was the calendar year, not the typed dates). Three tests that pinned `toBeDisabled` were replaced by four pinning the enterable state and the ignored range `[app/bms/CORPT00.bms:L127-L193; app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L149, L212-L256]` |
| Switching the report window clears the standing rejection, its `aria-invalid` marking and re-places the cursor on the option just chosen | Leave the rejection until the next ENTER | A rejection belongs to the turn that produced it; changing the selection changes the very input that turn rejected. Leaving it also left focus on `BODY` after the click, so the next keystroke went nowhere | None; verified at runtime that no `role="alert"` remains, the group's `aria-invalid` attribute is removed from the DOM, and `document.activeElement` is the chosen radio |
| `/reports` has no `F4=Clear` key | Add one for consistency with `/billpay` and the card screens | `CORPT00.bms:L226` declares the row-24 legend as exactly `'ENTER=Continue  F3=Back'`, and `CORPT00C:L184-L195` handles only `DFHENTER` and `DFHPF3`. Adding a third key would be an affordance the mapset does not declare. The screen's own reset is the `N` confirmation, which performs `INITIALIZE-ALL-FIELDS` | A tester expecting the key will not find it; confirmed byte-exact against the mapset `[app/bms/CORPT00.bms:L226; app/cbl/CORPT00C.cbl:L184-L195]` |
| The balance inquiry and the payment remain ONE endpoint whose behaviour depends on `confirm` | Split into `GET /billpay/{acctId}` for the inquiry and `POST /billpay` for the payment | `COBIL00C` is one CICS transaction and one screen turn for both; the only difference is the value of `CONFIRMI` in the same `RECEIVE MAP`. A single endpoint parameterised by `confirm` is the faithful mapping of that turn, and splitting it would invent a second turn the program does not have. The inquiry is provably non-mutating (`transactionId: null`, account version unchanged) and the endpoint is CSRF-protected | A reader of the API may expect an inquiry to be a `GET`; the request shape makes the distinction explicit `[app/cbl/COBIL00C.cbl:L155-L245]` |

## 43. QA Remediation — User Screens (`COUSR00` / `COUSR01` / `COUSR02` / `COUSR03`)

Findings raised against the four administrator user screens. Each row is settled from the mapset and the
program rather than from the finding text, and every decision below was re-verified at runtime.

### 43.1 The look-ahead read that decides whether a further page exists

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The user-list read fetches `PAGE_SIZE + 1` rows, reports "a further page exists" from whether the eleventh row came back, and returns only ten | Keep reporting a further page whenever the page came back full | `COUSR00C.PROCESS-PAGE-FORWARD` fills ten rows and then performs ONE further `READNEXT`, setting `NEXT-PAGE-YES` only when that eleventh record exists (`L307-L316`). Deriving the flag from "the page is full" is a different predicate, and it differs on exactly the case a positioned browse produces: a search whose result fills the page but has nothing after it. The screen then offered a forward key the server would refuse, and the refusal arrived as an HTTP 400 rather than as the screen's own message | One extra row is read per page. The flag is now the source's flag, so `PF8` is offered if and only if the eleventh record exists — pinned by four tests, two of them for the exactly-one-page case, and verified at the wire (`?userId=QAPAGE` returns ten rows with `nextPage false`) |
| A browse that fails leaves the rows already on screen, holding the last successful response in page state | Let the failure clear the rows, as the shared request hook does by default | Both of `COUSR00C`'s paging-boundary branches publish their literal with `SET SEND-ERASE-NO TO TRUE` and re-send the map (`L252-L253`, `L275-L276`), so the painted rows survive a boundary message. Clearing them destroyed a browse position that the message never claimed had moved, leaving a header, a stale `Page:` counter and no rows | The screen can show rows from the previous response beside a fresh failure message, which is precisely the legacy rendering. The line-23 banner still reads from the CURRENT response, so a stale success can never reappear beside a fresh failure |
| A boundary key is answered by the screen itself, with no request at all | Send the key and let the service answer | The `ELSE` branch of `PROCESS-PF8-KEY` (`L270-L276`) and of `PROCESS-PF7-KEY` (`L248-L253`) performs no `STARTBR` and no `READNEXT` — it moves the literal and re-sends. Answering locally is the faithful mapping, and it is why zero network traffic is the correct observation at a boundary | The genuine server-side boundary guard is retained for a cursor that arrives at the end of the file, so the rule is enforced in both places. Verified at runtime: the boundary `F8` issued ZERO requests, proven twice over (fetch/XHR interception and the resource timeline) |
| A successful forward turn onto the terminal page still publishes `'You have reached the bottom of the page...'` | Publish nothing, since the turn succeeded | That literal is not a boundary refusal: it lives in `READNEXT-USER-SEC-FILE`'s `WHEN DFHRESP(ENDFILE)` branch (`L637`), which is reached by the look-ahead read itself. A forward turn that lands on the last page therefore always carries it | None; it is a different literal from the `'You are already at the bottom of the page...'` refusal (`L273`) and both were observed in their own case |

### 43.2 The password is optional on the update screen

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| A blank password on `/users/update` means "leave the stored credential alone"; the client omits the member and the service skips the credential entirely | Reproduce `COUSR02C`'s `'Password can NOT be empty...'` edit literally | `COUSR02C` re-displayed `SEC-USR-PWD` in `PASSWDO` on the ENTER turn, so by the time `PF5` ran the field was ALWAYS populated: the presence edit (`L177-L213`) guarded a field the program itself had filled, and the field-by-field compare (`L219-L234`) then found it equal. A one-way hash cannot be re-displayed, so the field arrives empty on exactly the turns where the legacy field arrived unchanged. Reproducing the edit literally made a name-only update impossible AND put the no-change guard (`L239-L241`) permanently out of reach | The password can no longer be cleared by clearing the field, which is correct for a credential. `aria-required` is therefore emitted on the four presence-edited fields and NOT on the password, so assistive technology is told the truth about which fields the turn requires. Verified end to end: a `PUT` body of exactly `{"firstName","lastName","userType"}` returned 200, the stored `{bcrypt}` hash was still 68 characters of the same shape afterwards, the user still signed on with the original password, and a repeat save then reported `'Please modify to update ...'` |
| "Changed" for the credential means the supplied value does not already match the stored hash | Treat any supplied value as a change | Encoding the same password produces a different hash every time, so a supplied-value comparison would report a change on every save and make the no-change guard unreachable for the second time. `passwordEncoder.matches` is the only comparison that answers the question `COUSR02C` was asking with `IF PASSWDI NOT = SEC-USR-PWD` | One hash comparison per save that supplies a password |

### 43.3 No value-set edit on the user type

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The service applies NO value-set edit to the user type; an unrecognised code reaches the write and is refused there | Keep the `'User Type must be A or U'` rejection | `COUSR01C.PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` (`L117-L151`) and `COUSR02C.UPDATE-USER-INFO` (`L177-L213`) are five-way PRESENCE edits and nothing more — no value-set edit on the user type exists anywhere in the legacy, and the literal appeared in no program. It was invented observable output, which the spec-literal rule forbids | An unrecognised code now surfaces as the write-failure literal the source does publish — `'Unable to Add User...'` / `'Unable to Update User...'` — which is less specific. Verified: the client transmits `"userType":"X"` unedited, the response is `400 'Unable to Add User...'` with `fieldErrors: null`, no row is written, and the retired literal appears nowhere in the DOM **or in the 396 KB served bundle** |
| The database `CHECK (sec_usr_type IN ('A','U'))` is retained rather than relaxed to match the legacy's willingness to store any character | Drop the constraint so an arbitrary code round-trips as it did on VSAM | `SessionContext.UserType.fromCode` returns `null` for an unknown code, so a stored `'X'` would produce a null user type at every downstream read — a worse outcome than refusing the write. The constraint is what makes the refusal happen at all, and it is what makes the reachable legacy literal reachable | A code the legacy would have stored is refused. The refusal text is the legacy's own, and the schema constraint is named in the `updateUser` and `addUser` documentation so the cause is discoverable `[db/migration/V1__create_schema.sql:L96-L97]` |

### 43.4 A repeated Delete is discarded, not queued

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| An in-flight DELETE is tracked in a ref set synchronously, before anything can yield, and a second activation returns immediately | Rely on the `deleting` state flag; disable the control while deleting | A 3270 keyboard is locked from the moment an AID is sent until the program replies, so a second `PF5` struck in the meantime is DISCARDED rather than queued. The state flag cannot express that: several activations dispatched within one JavaScript task all observe it still `false`, so each issues its own DELETE — the first removing the record and the rest reporting it missing, which is what the finding observed. Disabling the control is also wrong here, for the reason in the next row | None; the ref is released in a `finally`. Verified with three `.click()` calls dispatched **0.8 ms apart inside one task**, with the control sampled `disabled === false` before every one: exactly ONE `DELETE` returning 204, one confirmation on the informational channel, and neither failure literal anywhere in the document. The same holds for three legend-button clicks and three `F5` keydowns in one task |
| The Delete affordance stays enabled when no record is loaded | Disable it until a record has been read | `COUSR03C` dispatches `WHEN DFHPF5 PERFORM DELETE-USER-INFO` unconditionally — there is no is-a-record-loaded guard. `DELETE-USER-INFO` (`L174-L192`) applies ONE edit, the empty-key `'User ID can NOT be empty...'`, and then reads and deletes. Disabling would invent an affordance state the mapset does not declare AND make two reachable literals unreachable from the pointer path | A user can press Delete with nothing loaded and get a refusal. Both refusals were verified: the empty key produces its literal with the cursor on the field and ZERO network traffic, and an unknown id produces `'User ID NOT found...'` on a 404 |
| `'Unable to Update User...'` on the DELETE screen is correct, not a copy-paste error | Reword it to mention deletion | `COUSR03C.DELETE-USER-SEC-FILE`'s `WHEN OTHER` branch moves exactly that literal (`L332`). The screen's own three outcomes are the deletion confirmation, `'User ID NOT found...'`, and this one | None; it now appears only on a genuine write failure, and was absent from every observed run |

### 43.5 Colour, geometry and structure literals on the four mapsets

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `Enter User ID:` is GREEN on `/users/update` and `/users/delete`, using the `.green` utility introduced for `COBIL00` | Leave both on the shared turquoise `.prompt` class | `COUSR02.bms:L80-L84` and `COUSR03.bms:L80-L84` both declare that caption `LENGTH=14 POS=(6,6) COLOR=GREEN`. `COUSR00`'s `Search User ID:` is a different literal at a different position and IS turquoise, so the three are not interchangeable | None; verified as exactly `rgb(51,255,102)` on both screens against the turquoise captions beside them |
| The 70-character rule on `/users/update` is YELLOW | Leave it in the default tone | `COUSR02.bms:L94-L95` declares the run `COLOR=YELLOW LENGTH=70 POS=(8,6)`, the same as `COUSR03`'s, which already carried the class | None; verified as `rgb(255,210,26)` with `textContent.length` exactly 70 and a single distinct character |
| A blank terminal row is restored between the entry row and the rule, by giving the shared rule group `margin-top: var(--term-line)` | Add the gap only where the focus ring was clipped | All five mapsets that paint a 70-character rule put the entry row at row 6 and the rule at `POS=(8,…)` — `COBIL00:L94-L95`, `COUSR02:L94-L95`, `COUSR03:L94-L95`, `COTRN01:L96-L97`, `COTRN02:L113-L114`. Row 7 is blank on every one of them, so the gap belongs to the shared rule rather than to one screen. It is also the root cause of the reported overlap: the ring had nowhere to be drawn | The rule moves down one line on five screens, three of them signed off in earlier phases, so it is on the cross-cutting re-check list. Verified as **20.797px** — one terminal line to within 0.003px — with the focused-field ring clearing the rule by 18.796px and the focused-button ring by 17.796px, neither overlapping |
| The list table declares its five column widths from the BMS rule runs (3, 8, 20, 20, 4 at columns 5, 12, 24, 48, 72) and is held to their total of 65 character cells | Let the table lay itself out from its content | Those runs ARE the column widths on a 3270. Under content-driven layout a narrow viewport shrank the columns until `overflow: hidden` clipped surnames mid-word and allocated the width-less last column nothing at all — a zero-width column adds nothing to the scrollable range, so scrolling could not reveal it either. This is the same fix already applied to the other two list screens | The table exceeds a 375px viewport and the shortfall becomes scroll range in the shared `.tableScroll` container, which carries a thin green scrollbar, a hover brightening and a focus-visible outline, and is keyboard-scrollable at `tabIndex=0`. Verified: the PAGE never scrolls horizontally at 1440/768/375/320; `table-layout` is `fixed`; the floor measures 65.05–65.12 ch; and at maximum scroll the `Type` header text and all ten `Type` values are fully inside the container with no `Last Name` cell clipped |
| The `(A=Admin, U=User)` hint on `/users/delete` sits OUTSIDE the description list, in a plain wrapper beside it | Keep it inside the `dl` with the caption and value it shares a row with | `COUSR03` row 15 paints the caption at column 6, the value at column 17 and the hint at column 19, so all three share one terminal row — but a `dl` may contain only `dt`/`dd` groups (optionally wrapped in `div`), and a bare element between them is the structure violation an audit reports | None. Verified: three `dl` elements, each with exactly one `div.detailField` child containing `[dt, dd]`; the hint's seven-level ancestor chain contains no `dl` (`closest('dl') === null`); Lighthouse accessibility **100** with `definition-list` and `dlitem` both passing and **zero** failing audits of 76 |
| `/users/add` is wrapped in a labelled `section`, and the dead hidden submit control on `/users/update` was removed | Leave the body outside every landmark; keep the hidden button as a form-submit fallback | A screen body that sits outside every landmark is unreachable by landmark navigation, and the other screens already publish one. The hidden button was reachable by nothing: it had no visible affordance, and the form's own `onSubmit` already carries ENTER | None. Verified: exactly one `section` exposed as `region "Add User"`, and **zero** `<button>` elements of any kind inside `<main>` on `/users/update`, with no submit-capable or zero-size control anywhere in the document |
| Only the FIRST selected row is marked when a `Sel` value is rejected | Mark every row carrying a bad value | `COUSR00C.PROCESS-ENTER-KEY` is an `EVALUATE TRUE` over `SEL0001`..`SEL0010` (`L151-L186`) and is first-match-wins — a second entry further down the page is never inspected, and the program has no message for it. Marking it would assert a rejection the program never made | A user who typed two bad values sees one marked. Verified: with `X` in row 2 and `Z` in row 4, the document-wide `[aria-invalid="true"]` set is exactly `["SEL0002"]`, row 4 carries neither attribute, and both keep the normal green frame |

### 43.6 A rejected field is marked by cursor and flag, never by colour

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| None of the four user screens reddens a rejected entry field or writes a `*` marker into it; the marking is the cursor plus the line-23 message, with `aria-invalid` alongside | Apply the `.fieldError` treatment for consistency with `/cards/update` and `/accounts/update` | The census over the four programs is decisive: `COUSR00C` contains **zero** `MOVE DFHRED`, zero `MOVE '*'` and zero `MOVE DFHGREEN`; `COUSR01C` zero and zero, with one `DFHGREEN` (`L254`); `COUSR03C` the same with one `DFHGREEN` (`L317`); and `COUSR02C`'s single `DFHRED` (`L241`) colours **`ERRMSGC` — the message line itself, not a data field**. Every rejection on these screens is `MOVE <literal> TO WS-MESSAGE` plus `MOVE -1 TO <field>L`. A red frame is observable output the mapsets never declare | A sighted user distinguishes the rejected field by the focus outline rather than by colour, exactly as on the transaction, bill-payment and report screens. `aria-invalid` is not visible output and has no BMS analogue to contradict. Verified on all four screens: `aria-invalid="true"`, `className` exactly `field`, `border-color` `rgb(31,156,64)` on all four sides, and a document-wide `.fieldError` count of **zero** |
| An invalid `Sel` value places the cursor on the SEARCH field, not on the offending cell | Place it on the cell that was rejected | `COUSR00C` publishes the invalid-selection literal and then `MOVE -1 TO USRIDINL` (`L184`) — the cursor goes to `USRIDIN`. The rejected cell is identified by the message and by `aria-invalid`, which is what `aria-describedby` on that cell now carries | None; verified as `document.activeElement.id === 'USRIDIN'` with `SEL0002` describing itself by `screenMessageLine` |

### 43.7 The keyboard-locked interval, and the shell's skip link

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The list screen's entry fields are natively `disabled` while its read is in flight, which means focus rests on the document body for the duration of that read | Use `readOnly` with `aria-disabled` so the focused field keeps focus | A locked 3270 keyboard REFUSES input; it does not accept keystrokes and then overwrite them when the reply paints. `disabled` is the honest expression of that, and a disabled element cannot hold focus. `useFocusOnSettled` therefore places the cursor in the SAME commit that re-enables the field, which is the strongest guarantee available | Focus measurably rests on `BODY` for the length of the read — observed as 4 of 69 samples across an 11 ms window before settling on the correct field. The alternative would let keystrokes be silently discarded by the repaint, which is worse |
| The shell's document-level ENTER binding EXEMPTS elements that activate themselves on the key — `<button>` and any anchor with an `href` | Keep exempting buttons only; give the skip link a click handler of its own | The binding called `preventDefault()` for every other target, which suppressed the skip link's native activation: pressing ENTER on the focused link fired no `click` and no `hashchange`, threw focus back to the first form field, and re-ran the screen's ENTER action — issuing a redundant read. A skip link exists solely for the operator who has no pointer, so pointer-only operation makes it useless. The skip link is the ONLY anchor the application renders, so the exemption cannot reach a screen field | The exemption is element-type scoped and was isolated by reading `defaultPrevented` after the app handler ran: `false` for the anchor and for a legend button, `true` for an entry field. Verified on two unrelated routes — the keypress now produces a real `click` and `hashchange`, moves focus onto the footer region, and issues **zero** requests, while ENTER from an entry field still submits the screen and ENTER on a legend button still fires it exactly once |
| `footer#screenStatusRegion` carries `tabIndex={-1}` | Leave it unfocusable, or make it part of the tab sequence | An anchor targeting a non-focusable element moves the scroll position and the sequential starting point but leaves `document.activeElement` on the body, so the next Tab is the only evidence the jump happened. A negative index is focusable programmatically without joining the tab sequence, which is exactly what a skip target needs | None; verified that activation lands focus on the footer itself and the next Tab reaches the first legend button rather than a form control |


## 44. QA Remediation — Cross-Cutting Explainability Backlog

The QA pass measured this log and the traceability matrix against the delivered application and reported
AAP requirement #20 as a FAIL: sixteen deviations covered, twenty-two uncovered, one logged decision
contradicting both the running app and the legacy source, and one logged policy contradicted by three
rendered controls. Sections 40 through 43 closed the per-screen half of that backlog as each screen group
was remediated. This section closes the remainder — the cross-cutting decisions, the decisions taken
earlier in this same pass whose rationale had not yet been written down, and the two rows the report found
to be out of step with the code. Every row below is settled from the mapset, the program or a measurement,
and every one of them was verified at runtime before it was written here.

### 44.1 The responsive strategy a fixed 24x80 frame implies (AAP #18)

Nothing in this log described how a screen whose geometry is declared in character cells is expected to
behave in a viewport narrower than eighty of them, and the report was right that the strategy was
undocumented — a probe for the word `breakpoint` returned zero hits. The strategy is recorded here in full,
because it is the rule that decided six separate findings.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| A viewport narrower than the frame CONTAINS the shortfall in an internal scroller; the declared columns never reflow and the page frame itself never scrolls horizontally | Reflow the BMS columns into a narrow single-column stack at every width; let the document scroll horizontally; let the frame clip | A BMS field's meaning is partly its POSITION: `LENGTH=15 POS=(17,61) JUSTIFY=RIGHT` says "this is the right-hand money column", and a stack destroys that. Letting the document scroll is worse still, because rows 23 and 24 travel with it and the operator loses the message line and the key legend — the two rows that tell them what happened and what they may press. Containment keeps every declared column at its declared width AND keeps the two status rows pinned | Content genuinely wider than the viewport must be scrolled to. Every such region is therefore a real, discoverable scroller: `.tableScroll` carries a thin green scrollbar, a hover brightening, a `:focus-visible` ring and `tabIndex=0` so it is keyboard-operable, and `.screen__body` absorbs the rest. Verified across 17 routes x 4 widths: `documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` everywhere, and `window.scrollY === 0` after `scrollTo(0, 9999)` on 36 of 36 route/viewport combinations |
| Continuous CSS functions (`min()`, `clamp()`, `minmax()`) are preferred to a media query wherever the quantity degrades continuously; the stylesheet declares only THREE media conditions — `48rem`, `32rem` and `prefers-reduced-motion` | Add a conventional five-breakpoint ladder and re-declare the affected values in each tier | A breakpoint states that a value is correct on one side and wrong on the other, which is true of a LAYOUT change and false of a length. The sign-on banner's declared 20-column offset, for instance, is correct at every width where there is room for it and wrong only in the last few pixels: `min(20ch, 100% - 42ch)` yields the offset continuously and preserves the mapset's declared indent wherever it fits, which a breakpoint override would discard wholesale below its threshold. Fewer tiers also means fewer places a declared value can drift out of step with the mapset | A reader looking for a breakpoint ladder will not find one. Verified that the continuous form is exact at the edges: the sign-on indent measures 20.000ch at 1440 and 19.999ch at 768, then exactly `clientWidth - artWidth` at 375 (58.17px) and 320 (3.17px), with zero characters clipped at any of the four |
| The two media tiers that DO exist are structural, and each also steps the terminal font down one size: `48rem` -> 14px, `32rem` -> 12px | Keep the 16px cell at every width and rely on scrolling alone; step the font without the structural changes | The cell size is the frame's unit, so stepping it is the only change that makes MORE of the eighty columns fit rather than merely reachable. Each tier pairs that with the structural change the width forces: at `48rem` the header row becomes a wrapping flex row so a caption and its value move down together, and the browse cells lose a character of padding on each edge; at `32rem` the caption/field grids of the search and update screens collapse to one column, because a 20-character caption and a 50-character field cannot both fit and the caption would otherwise scroll off to the left | Three font tiers mean three sets of measured geometry, so every measurement in this log records the tier it was taken at (1ch = 9.594px / 8.401px / 7.188px). Verified at all four widths on all 17 routes |
| The 24-row floor is `min-height: min(calc(var(--term-rows) * var(--term-line)), 100vh)`, not the bare calculation | Keep the unconditional floor and cap it with `max-height: 100vh`; add a media query for short viewports | The bare floor plus a cap does not work, and this was found at runtime rather than reasoned about: per CSS a USED `min-height` outranks a smaller `max-height`, so the cap was inert whenever the viewport was wider than 48rem AND shorter than 499.2px. The frame then grew past the window, the page scrolled, and rows 23 and 24 left the viewport by 146px and 178px. Folding the cap INTO the floor removes the conflict in one declaration | A viewport shorter than 24 rows shows fewer than 24 rows' worth of body, with the remainder in the internal scroller — which is the only honest outcome. Verified over 22 viewport heights: 10/10 at 1440 x {900..240} and 12/12 across {800, 769, 768, 320} x {480, 400, 300}, with the branch switch visible in the data (the computed floor pins at 499.2px above 500px of height and equals `innerHeight` below it). Proven load-bearing by A/B — re-injecting the unconditional floor reproduced the defect exactly |

### 44.2 The keyboard lock, the operator information area, and what line 24 does underneath it

The report's third area of concern was that the application had no visible loading affordance anywhere and
disabled no control during a fetch, which is what produced the stale-data window and the request
coalescing. The remediation models the 3270 keyboard lock rather than inventing a spinner, and the
decisions that follow from that are recorded here.

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| The busy state is reported on a reserved row BELOW the twenty-four application rows — the Operator Information Area — which carries `X SYSTEM` for exactly as long as the keyboard is locked | Render a spinner or progress bar; put the wait on line 23; leave the wait invisible, as the app did before this pass | The OIA is where a real terminal reports input inhibition, and it is not one of the 24 rows, so using it consumes no BMS field and changes no screen's declared geometry. `X SYSTEM` is the terminal's own literal for the condition, so it is not invented text. Line 23 was rejected because it would overwrite the program's own message, and a spinner because it is observable output with no analogue anywhere in the source | The row is reserved whether or not it carries text, so no screen's height changes when a request starts. Verified in flight: `768.14 x 20.8` at the correct position, turquoise and bold, `role="status" aria-live="polite"`, present in 149 of 149 samples across a throttled window and empty again after settling |
| A repeat of the SAME attention identifier is refused SYNCHRONOUSLY by a per-AID latch, ahead of the rendered lock | Rely on the rendered `disabled` state alone; debounce on a timer | The rendered lock is committed view state, so it can only refuse a key that arrives in a LATER task than the one that sent the previous AID. Five `PF8` presses inside one task all read a screen that has not yet been told it is busy, and each sends its own request carrying the cursor of the page still displayed — which is exactly what the report measured. A 3270 discards those keystrokes at the terminal, before the program ever sees them, and only a synchronous latch expresses that. A timer would additionally refuse a legitimate second press | The latch releases on the next microtask unless the AID actually started a transaction, so a key that only repaints or leaves the screen costs the operator nothing. Verified: five same-task `F8` keydowns produce exactly ONE request and one page turn, while two genuine presses produce two requests with strictly advancing cursors |
| An inhibited legend key is DIMMED with alpha, never recoloured | Let the generic `button:disabled` grey apply, as it did before | A BMS literal field does not change colour when the terminal locks — inhibition is reported on the OIA, never by repainting line 24. The generic grey repainted the ENTIRE legend `rgb(90, 90, 90)` for the duration of every send, which is a defect this pass introduced itself by combining the busy lock with the row-24 colour census, and found by measuring rather than by review | Verified over 624 genuinely in-flight samples under throttling: the declared hue held with a single distinct value on both the yellow and the turquoise screens, the grey buckets were empty, and the rendered pixels came back at exactly 0.55x the declared colour per channel |
| Line 23's live region is REMOUNTED once per send, so a repeated identical message is announced again and the cursor is re-placed | Leave the region mounted and let the framework skip an identical string | A terminal re-sends the whole map on every turn, so the second identical refusal is a fresh event to the operator: the message is repainted and the cursor is moved again. A live region whose text has not changed announces nothing, so the second refusal was silent to a screen reader and left the cursor wherever it happened to be | The region's DOM node is replaced per send, which a test asserting node identity across turns would notice. Verified with a MutationObserver: on the second identical rejection the alert element is REMOVED and a new one with the same text ADDED, and focus moves back onto the rejected field |
| The header's `Date:` and `Time:` are re-captured on every send rather than ticking | Tick from a timer; capture once at mount | `POPULATE-HEADER-INFO` runs immediately before every `SEND MAP`, so the legacy clock advances per turn and is otherwise frozen. A ticking clock is a different, invented behaviour; capturing once is wrong in the other direction | Verified both ways: unchanged across 13s and 12s of idle (14/14 and 12/12 identical samples) and advanced by +90s and +77s on the next send, matching the server `date` header to the second |

### 44.3 Focus, hover, diagnostics and the tooling that polices them

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| A pointer-activated legend key does NOT retain focus; roughly 39ms later the screen re-places the cursor on the field its mapset declares | Keep focus on the activated key so the pointer user can press it again with ENTER | The mapset's cursor placement wins on AAP precedence. `app/bms/COTRN01.bms:L85` declares `TRNIDIN ATTRB=(FSET,IC,NORM,UNPROT)` and `COTRN01C` ends EVERY path with `MOVE -1 TO TRNIDINL` (`L102`, `L151`, `L154`, `L287`, `L294`, `L311`) — the cursor is on the entry field after each turn, without exception. Retaining focus on chrome is a focus-management nicety with no BMS analogue, and it would contradict a declared attribute | A pointer user must click the key again rather than press ENTER. Recorded rather than changed, because the alternative changes observable behaviour the source specifies. The key's own focus ring is correct while it holds focus — `solid 2px rgb(255, 210, 26)` |
| The key legend is exposed as `role="group"`, not `role="toolbar"` | Keep `role="toolbar"`, which reads more naturally for a row of buttons | The ARIA toolbar pattern owes a roving `tabindex` and arrow-key plus Escape handling. Implementing it would REPLACE the mapset's declared tab order with a different one, and declaring the role without implementing the pattern is a false promise to assistive technology. A group with a label describes the row truthfully and leaves the tab order alone | Twelve test files assert on the role and were updated with it. Verified on all 17 routes: `role="group"` present on every one, and a document-wide `[role="toolbar"]` query returns zero elements |
| `jsx-a11y/no-noninteractive-tabindex` keeps its allow-list extended with the `group` and `region` roles rather than being disabled or worked around | Disable the rule; give the scroll container an interactive role it does not have; put the tab stop on a wrapper button | A horizontally scrolling region must be operable from the keyboard (WCAG 2.1.1), and the three browse tables are exactly that: on a narrow viewport the trailing BMS columns fall outside the frame and hold no focusable field of their own, so the container itself must carry the tab stop. The rule's default allow-list covers only `tabpanel`. Giving the container `role="toolbar"` or `role="button"` to satisfy the linter would misdescribe it, which is the worse trade | The allow-list is widened for the whole project rather than suppressed at each site, so a future non-scrolling `region` could take a tab stop without the linter objecting. The three sites are named in the config comment. This row replaces the `section 19.2` pointer that comment previously carried |
| `button:focus-visible` was widened to `button:focus` | Keep `:focus-visible` | A programmatically focused legend key — which is what happens when the screen re-places the cursor, and what a keyboard-only operator sees — does not always satisfy the browser's `:focus-visible` heuristic, so the ring was intermittently absent exactly when it mattered. The ring was also strengthened from 1px green to 2px yellow for contrast | A mouse click paints the ring for as long as the key holds focus. Verified: no `:focus-visible` rule for buttons remains in the served bundle, and a focused key renders `solid 2px rgb(255, 210, 26)` |
| Every hover treatment in the stylesheet is wrapped in `@media (hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)` | Leave the hover rules unconditional; clear them with a touch handler | On a touch device the last-tapped element retains `:hover` indefinitely, so a tapped browse row stayed in reverse video and read as SELECTED when nothing was. Reverse video is meaningful on a 3270 — it is what `HILIGHT` declares — so a false one is a false statement about the screen. The media condition removes the treatment where the pointer cannot hover at all | Verified under genuine touch emulation: with the pointer still literally over the row, `tr.matches(':hover')` was TRUE while the computed background stayed transparent, which proves the gate rather than merely observing the absence. A scan for `:hover` selectors outside a hover-capable condition returns an empty list |
| An API failure that carries no `message` renders the backend's OWN generic literal, `An unexpected error occurred`, and never an HTTP-client diagnostic | Render `error.message`, as the client did before; render a client-authored fallback sentence | `Request failed with status code 400`, `Network Error` and `AxiosError` are library strings. Line 23 is a BMS field that carries program messages, so a library diagnostic there is invented observable output — and it also leaks the transport in use to the operator. Reusing the backend's own `GlobalExceptionHandler` literal character-for-character means the sentence on line 23 is one the system already publishes | The constant is duplicated in the frontend and must stay byte-identical to the backend's; it lives in its own module so a test that mocks the API layer still resolves it. Verified offline and against three upstream 400s with empty envelopes: line 23 read exactly the generic sentence, with zero hits for any library string |
| The HTTP client carries a 30-second timeout | Leave requests untimed | The keyboard lock is released when the request settles. An untimed request that never settles locks the terminal permanently, with `X SYSTEM` displayed and every key refused — the lock this pass introduced turned a hung request into an unrecoverable screen | A slow-but-legitimate request is abandoned at 30s and reported through the generic message path |
| `document.title` is `"<tran> <program> - CardDemo"`, built from the transaction id and program name each screen already publishes | Use the two BMS title lines; use the screen heading | The two title lines are IDENTICAL on all 17 mapsets, so they identify the application and cannot distinguish one screen from another in a browser tab or a history entry. The transaction id and program name are the pair the legacy itself uses to identify a screen, and both are already carried in the chrome contract | Verified distinct and correct on all 17 routes, each suffixed ` - CardDemo` |

### 44.4 Sign-on and the two menu screens

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `GET /session` is `permitAll` and answers 200 with an empty identity, instead of being gated and answering 401 | Keep the route gated; suppress the console error on the client; probe a different route | "Nobody is signed on" is a truthful answer to "who is signed on", not a refusal, so the route is a state query rather than a guarded resource. The 401 also could not be handled away: axios uses XHR, and Chrome logs `Failed to load resource: 401` for a non-2xx XHR regardless of how the script handles the rejection, so EVERY cold page load reported a console error to any diagnostic collector. That the client already treated a 200 with a missing identity as signed-out is strong evidence this was the intended contract all along | The route now answers anonymously, so it must disclose nothing: it reads only the caller's own session, returns a private static record with both members null, and mints no session. Three gateway tests were aligned to the corrected behaviour, one of them still asserting that the probe creates no session. Verified: unauthenticated `200 {"userId":null,"userType":null}`, and a cold unauthenticated load reports ZERO console messages at any level with Lighthouse best-practices at 100 |
| `SysID` is sourced from the single application identity `CARDDEMO`, so `AppID:` and `SysID:` both read `CARDDEMO` | Leave it unset so the mapset's blank `INITIAL` shows; invent a four-character region name | The build previously left it empty on the theory that the blank default was faithful, which misread the source: `COSGN00.bms` does declare `SYSID INITIAL='        '`, but `COSGN00C:L202-L203` ALWAYS performs `EXEC CICS ASSIGN SYSID` before the first `SEND`, so the field is never blank in front of an operator. `CARDDEMO` is the one application identity that actually exists in the repository (`app/csd/CARDDEMO.CSD`) and it fits either `LENGTH=8` field exactly; inventing a region name would breach the spec-literal "no invented names" rule | A real deployment's region name differs, so the value is overridable through the image build argument. Verified: both header cells non-empty and equal to `CARDDEMO` |
| `targetRoute` was REMOVED from the menu wire contract, and the `PF3` branch now publishes `programName = COSGN00C` | Correct the route table so the server's directive matches the SPA's; keep the field and continue to ignore it | The field mapped each program to the gateway's own downstream SERVICE PREFIX, not to a screen, so it disagreed with the SPA on three of its values and gave all four administrator options the identical `/users`. Nothing consumed it — the SPA routes from its own program-to-screen table — so it was unrequested wire output that actively contradicted the application, and a trap for any future reader who assumed the server directed navigation. Correcting it instead would have made the server the second authority on routing, which is a design the AAP does not ask for. `COSGN00C` is the actual `CDEMO-TO-PROGRAM` value both menus move before returning to sign-on (`COMEN01C:L96-L98`, `COADM01C:L96-L98`), so the `PF3` response now names a program rather than nothing at all | Both response types lost a member, and five frontend test files plus the shared types were updated. Verified at the wire: the key set is exactly `["dispatched", "programName", "message"]`, `targetRoute` appears in no body, and option 2 navigates to `/accounts/update` from `programName` alone |
| The sign-on note's declared 20-column indent is `min(20ch, 100% - 42ch)` | Override the indent below a breakpoint; shrink the art; let it clip | The art is nine lines of exactly 42 characters with `white-space: pre`, so the indent plus the art exceeded the content box by 85.8px at 375 and 140.8px at 320 — losing 12 and then 20 of the 42 characters behind an overlay scrollbar that advertised nothing. The continuous form yields the declared offset only as far as it must: CSS clamps a negative `calc` result to zero, so the indent can shrink to nothing but never invert, and the art is never the thing that gives way | Below roughly 512px the mapset's declared offset is partly or wholly absent, which is the lesser of the two infidelities. Verified at four widths with a per-character range measurement: all nine lines render their full 302.45px at both 375 and 320 |
| Header cells are `white-space: nowrap` at every width, and below `48rem` the header row becomes a wrapping flex row | Let the cells wrap internally; use `text-overflow: ellipsis`; keep the grid and add `nowrap` | The sign-on captions are spelled with a space before the colon — `Tran :`, `Prog :`, `Date :`, `Time :` (`COSGN00.bms:L33/L46/L56/L69`) — and that space is a break opportunity INSIDE a BMS literal, so at 320px a lone `:` took a line of its own. A caption and the value beside it are one terminal row and must never split. The grid could not move a whole cell to a new row, so `nowrap` alone would have pushed the frame past the viewport: `1fr` is `minmax(auto, 1fr)` and cannot shrink below min-content. A flex row can move the cell down intact | Row 1 is two lines tall below 48rem. Verified at 1440/375/320: every cell has `getClientRects().length === 1` and exactly one distinct text-line top, so no lone colon exists anywhere, and the total line count at 320 improved from five to four |
| The menu option field RETAINS `-` and `.` as the operator types, clamped to two characters | Strip everything but digits at entry, as the field did before | This is the decisive insight of the finding: a 3270 numeric-shift field admits a minus sign and a period alongside the digits, which is precisely WHY `COMEN01C:L127` tests `IF WS-OPTION IS NOT NUMERIC` rather than trusting the keyboard. Stripping them at entry is what created the defect — `-1` became `1` and was DISPATCHED, opening Account View, and `1.5` became `15` and reached the server as a 400. Retaining them lets the program's own edit refuse them, which is what the source does | A non-numeric character is briefly visible in the field before ENTER refuses it, exactly as on a terminal. Verified: `-1` retains its DOM value as `-1`, is not rewritten, and produces the option-number refusal with zero `menu/select` requests |
| The `COMEN01C` / `COADM01C` option edit is duplicated on the client, and a refused option never becomes a request | Send every option and let the server edit it; edit only on the client | The legacy edit runs in the same task as the send, so a refused option never becomes a file access. Reproducing it client-side keeps that property and gives the operator the screen's own message rather than an HTTP failure. The server edit is retained unchanged because the client is not a trust boundary. The comparison is against the TOTAL option count (10 and 4), not the role-filtered list length, because `COMEN01C:L128` uses `CDEMO-MENU-OPT-COUNT` and the role gate at `L137` is a separate edit with its own message | Two copies of one edit must stay in step; both cite the same source lines. Verified for `-1`, `1.`, `0`, `99` and empty: each produced exactly `Please enter a valid option number...` with `aria-invalid`, the cursor back on the field, and a total of ZERO select requests — proven by request-sequence numbering with no gap for a suppressed call |
| Option numbers are zero-padded to two digits at the render layer | Pad them in the gateway response; leave them unpadded | `COMEN02Y` declares `CDEMO-MENU-OPT-NUM PIC 9(02)`, so a `9(02)` field holding 1 stores `01`, and `COMEN01C:L243-L246` strings it `DELIMITED BY SIZE` — the legacy paints two digits and all ten option names align on one column. The wire carries an integer, so the padding is purely presentational; padding it server-side would change a numeric member into a string for a display concern. The helper deliberately does NOT truncate an over-wide value, because a lost digit changes the value | Verified by DOM range measurement: the option name begins at x = 374.344 for ALL TEN rows, a spread of 0.000px, and the four administrator rows likewise |
| A server-rejected option value faults its control, while a transport or server FAILURE faults nothing | Fault the control on any error; fault nothing until the client itself rejects | A 400 is a judgement about the value the operator typed, so the control carries `aria-invalid`; a 500 or a dropped connection judges no value, and marking the field would assert a rejection nobody made. The predicate is therefore `status === 400` specifically, not "an error occurred" | Verified: a server-refused option carries `aria-invalid="true"` and `aria-describedby`, and the generic-failure path marks nothing |

### 44.5 The account screens — geometry, cell sizing and the frame's own layout model

| Decision | Alternatives | Rationale | Risk |
|----------|--------------|-----------|------|
| `COACTVW` is rebuilt as the genuine 80-column TWO-COLUMN grid its mapset declares — left captions at column 8 with values at column 23, right captions at column 39 (`LENGTH=21`) with values at column 61 (`LENGTH=15 JUSTIFY=RIGHT PICOUT='+ZZZ,ZZZ,ZZZ.99'`) — and the row pitch is reconciled to one terminal line | Keep the single-column caption/value stack; use a two-column grid only above a breakpoint | The screen was 1036px tall in a 900px viewport — 2.03x the 24-row budget — so it SCROLLED and rows 23 and 24 sat below the fold, which is the AAP's 24x80 contract broken outright. The cause was not responsive: the markup was a single column at every width, so each of the mapset's paired rows consumed two. Transcribing the declared columns halves the row count and puts the money values on the one right-hand column the mapset gives them | The grid is now positional, so a caption or value whose declared column changes must be changed here too; each is cited to its `POS=`. Verified: the frame fell 1036px -> 510px with no scroll at 1440x900 and rows 23/24 inside the fold, all 28 value boxes at exact BMS columns with integer character spans, and the five money decimal points aligned to a spread of **0.000px** (from 28.813px) |
| The shell is a flex column whose BODY scrolls, and the 24-row frame is measured as the body's own leftover rather than as the frame height minus the sum of its children | Let the frame grow and the document scroll; measure with the direct-children formula | `.screen__body` computes to `flex: 1 1 auto`, so it absorbs all free space and the direct-children formula is identically zero REGARDLESS of content — a live counterfactual measured 0.0000 in both a one-row and a three-row state, so the formula could not have detected the `/accounts` overflow at all. Making the body the scroller is also what keeps rows 23 and 24 pinned while an over-tall body is still fully reachable | Content taller than the body is scrolled to inside the frame. Positively exercised rather than merely tolerated: `/accounts/:id/update` overflows its body by +55px at 1440x900 and **+281px** at 320x568 and still holds both status rows on screen; a two-line row-23 message and a two-row key legend each shrink the body to absorb themselves |
| An entry field's frame is a `border`, and the focus ring is an `outline` | Draw the frame as an outline and the focus ring as a border; use `box-shadow` for one of them | The two must be able to coexist, because a rejected field is frequently also the focused field — that is the whole point of moving the cursor to it. Both drawn as outlines means the focus ring REPLACES the red frame and the fault becomes invisible exactly when the operator is looking at it. Border for the persistent state and outline for the transient one keeps `.fieldError`'s red visible underneath the yellow ring | A border participates in layout, so every character-cell width is computed with `box-sizing: border-box`. Verified by pixel sampling across the field edge: yellow ring, then red frame, then content, then red, then yellow |
| Character-cell fields are sized from the declared `LENGTH` in `ch` units with `box-sizing: border-box`, `padding: 0`, a negative one-pixel inline start margin to absorb the border, and `max-width: none` | Size them in pixels; leave the shared `max-width: 100%` in force; use `size=` alone | A field declared `LENGTH=11` must be exactly eleven cells wide, or the column it starts is wrong for every screen that carries it. The shared `input { max-width: 100% }` silently clamped the field back to eleven cells MINUS the two border pixels, which clipped the eleventh glyph and left the field internally scrolled by 2px — a defect invisible to review and found only by typing eleven characters and reading `scrollWidth` | `max-width: none` is a deliberate escape from a global guard, so the width must come from the `ch` calculation and nothing else. Verified: content box 105.609375px = 11.008 track-`ch`, `scrollWidth <= clientWidth`, `scrollLeft` 0 when typed and after both Home and End, exactly eleven complete glyph runs in a 4x crop, and the field's left edge at exactly column 38 (delta 0.000000px) — identical on all four screens that carry it. A/B with the clamp re-injected reproduced the clipping |
| The `Zip` value on the account screens renders FIVE characters, not the ten the record holds | Render all ten; widen the field | `ACSZIPC` is declared `LENGTH=5 JUSTIFY=(RIGHT)` at `POS=(17,73)` while `CUST-ADDR-ZIP` is `PIC X(10)`, so the `MOVE` into the symbolic-map field truncates and the screen shows the first five characters.
